As he put it to Pope’s wife Mary, after the meeting, he had set out to prove her husband wrong (in his rejection of the travelling ‘photon’) but had ‘ended-up proving him right’ [77].

    In fine, then, there can be no truth in the age-old concept of light as a travelling intermediary between the atoms of objects. Together with its ancillary theoretical paraphernalia of ‘electrodynamics’, ‘æther’, and so on, that concept now appears to be no more than an obstruction in the path of scientific progress. As mentioned in Chapter 4, a logical alternative briefly appeared in the history of light theory with Bishop Robert Grosseteste and Roger Bacon in the 13$^{th}$ century. This was that what we commonly call ‘light’ is, in modern parlance, a holistic (i.e., holographic) array or pattern of quantum pixels, from which, in observation, we project the distances of physical objects from ourselves and one another in the way a television viewer projects the length, depth, breadth and action of a video scenario from patterns and sequences of lighted dots on a screen. This alternative to the traditional conception of light has been called ‘the cinematic model’ in Normal Realism.

    As for the experimentally proven ‘velocity’ aspect of light-communication, this is explained in ‘cinematic’ terms as what perception psychologists call the ‘phi phenomenon’, as illustrated in advertising, where the impression of motion is created by sequences of light-bulbs lit in rapid succession (see Chapter 5, section 5.3). In these terms, what we call a ‘light-ray’, as seen, for example in the case of rays from the setting Sun or a laser beam shone through a misty or smoky atmosphere, is no more than a sequence of illuminated mist or smoke particles without which – that is, in a pure vacuum – the ‘beam’, even of the strongest laser, would be completely invisible. (It is to be remembered that in this sequence, the transfer of energy from molecule to molecule within its own intrinsic, or ‘proper’ time, is instantaneous while in relative (observer) time that same transfer takes place at the delayed distance-time rate c.)

    In the following chapter we apply this new knowledge of light to physics in general, revealing just how obstructive this conceptual ‘spanner in the works’ has been to scientific progress and how unnecessary it is for it to remain. We begin that chapter by showing just how easily and naturally the whole issue of Quantum Theory and Relativity Theory could have been understood if light had not been so summarily interpreted in the way it has, as a space travelling enigma.

    CHAPTER 7

    Relativity Without Light-Speed

    In the previous chapter it was demonstrated that there is no sensible way in which light can be conceived as a space-travelling physical intermediary between objects and our perceptions of them, and that the constant $c$, which is commonly interpreted – in Einsteinian relativity, for instance – as the ‘velocity of light in vacuo’ is really no such thing but is nothing more nor less than a dimensional constant stating the ratio of conventional metres to conventional seconds in the measuring of optical distance (300,000 kilometres to the second in the Metric convention and 186,000 miles to the second in Imperial). In this way, that ratio of units tells us that in observational perspective, optical distance and time are not different measures but are one and the same measure. In this present chapter we show just how simply a quantised version of time dilation in Einstein’s Special Theory of Relativity may be deduced on that Neo-Machian basis by means of nothing more than Pythagoras’ Theorem applied to the four observational dimensions. This by-passes altogether, the tortuous academic history of concepts, not only of ‘light-velocity’ but also, consequently, of the ‘æther’, ‘electrodynamics’, ‘field-forces’, and so on. In later chapters, this same simplifying and conceptually clean-shaving ‘Ockham’s Razor’ approach is applied to other ‘traditional’ theories including Einstein’s General Theory of Relativity. As may be appreciated, all this is on the basis of Mach’s prototype relativism rather than that of his successor, Einstein.

    7.1. The polar dimensions of observational perspective

    In Machian relativity as in its Einsteinian successor, all physical dimensions are basically observer-centred. In Normal Realism, this means that they are spherical or polar. These four polar dimensions are: (i) azimuth or horizontal angle; (ii) elevation, or vertical angle; (iii) range, or radial distance, and, (iv) time, which is that radial distance in metres divided by $c$, or seconds.

    Like all three other dimensions, this fourth polar dimension, time, is measured at the observational centre, or pole. In this way, $c$ becomes the rate of temporal ‘contraction’, as it were, of the whole three-dimensional, spherical observer-frame towards its centre, where the various radial strands, or world-lines, of objects are, so to speak, woven into a rope, as indicated in Fig. 1 below.

    Fig. 1. The four-dimensional spherical observer-frame

    Within that spatio-temporal polar frame there are, of course, other centres, or point-perspectives of that same kind, each with its own similarly projected spherical frame. These other centres are not necessarily human. They may be objects of any sort, such as mirrors, cameras or other remote-viewing apparatuses. Anything of substance in that frame, animal, vegetable or mineral, is an ‘instrument’ in that sense, as either a source or relayer of informational data in the Machian meaning of the word.

    It is standard procedure in geometry to convert polar coordinates into the Cartesian or grid-type coordinates first envisaged by Descartes and then adopted, by Galileo, Newton et al. for the purposes of physics. By this means, any observer can extrapolate the positions of other interacting point-perspectives in the usual objective way that is familiar to us as the ordinary commonsense world extrapolated by people such as architects, surveyors, draughtsmen, geographers and the like – with the only exception being that of astronomers, for whom the polar frame is more or less obligatory. It is this Cartesian grid-system that we usually think of and talk about as ‘space’ in contexts of everyday commonsense experience (see Fig. 2). It is this Cartesian rectilinear frame-space that is initially assumed in Einstein’s Special Theory of Relativity, so it seems incongruous that although teachers of the subject talk about ‘observers’, ‘observer-frames’, and so on, they scarcely, if ever, make it clear what they mean by an ‘observer’ or by an ‘observer frame’, failing to emphasise to students the fact that, as observers, the first call in our knowledge of the whole objective

    Fig. 2. The secondary four-dimensional Cartesian frame

    scenario is each individual set of polar dimensions or perspectives described above. This omission perpetuates the impression that space is basically Cartesian and our observations of it no more than incidental. In Normal Realism that order of precedence is reversed, so that these polar spaces become epistemologically primary (a priori) and Cartesian space no more than secondary (a posteriori).

    Nevertheless, that Cartesian grid-space, or communal space, is the space that is, by definition, truly objective insofar as it decentralises the personal space and includes many observers, which makes the real world an objective community of perspectives. This is despite the fact that without those private polar spaces and their interactions with one another, involving objects complex or simple, there is no space, nor World to speak of. Between any one and any other of these points in that common objective space, as in polar space, all distances divided by $c$, in any direction, are times in seconds. This means that all bodies in that communal space are separated from one another in precisely the same way that they are in terms of ‘light-velocity’, except for the fact that no-one sees, or needs to see, anything travelling in the form either of waves or ‘photons’, far less in any form of conducting medium, such as a ‘field’ or ‘æther’. In this purely phenomenalistic, Neo-Machian account there is no more question as to how or by what supposed means these signals are conducted than there is about how our future birthdays arrive. The answer in both cases is that they occur simply ‘in the fullness of time’. In other words, the process is entirely datum-sequential or ‘cinematic’ in the Machian sense already described.

    It is to be emphasised, however, that this situation applies only for the rare case of observers remaining stationary relative to one another. As we shall see, where motion is involved, time is dilated, as described in section 7.3.

    7.2. Time and motion in Normal Realism

    What is time? ‘It’s funny stuff!’ one might say. However, plainly speaking, in the same way that length is what is measured by rulers, time is simply what is measured by clocks.

    The ultimate natural clocks are, of course, the atoms of which everything is composed, and there is no time more fundamental than this to which these clocks may be compared – and certainly not in the void that separates them. This means that there is no overall ‘God’s time’, as it were, ticking away continuously behind the scenes, far less in any kind of ‘God’s space’ in between bodies. Insofar as these ultimate atomic time-keepers are spatially separated, they are also temporally independent of one another. That is to say, since there is nothing ‘in the void’ to connect the time registered by any one of those clocks with that of any other, there is no overall or ‘universal’ synchrony, or time-plenum (‘God’s time’), that it makes sense to speak of.

    The ‘ticks’ of these atomic clocks are the quantum periods displayed in the spectra of these atoms and, as is well known, the atomic compositions of the different materials, together with their various other identifying characteristics, including speed of movement, etc., are as read in those digital ‘bar-codes’.

    But, still, time is ‘funny stuff’. In a fable told by the Greek slave, Aesop, in the 7$^{th}$ century B.C., a hare and a tortoise have a race. The hare contemptuously takes a nap during the first part of the race, thinking he can catch up and overtake the tortoise whenever he chooses. He is mortified, however, to find that at its very slow but steady speed the tortoise has pipped him to the post – hence the old saying: ‘Slow and steady wins the race’.

    Here is a modern, slightly modified version of that story. Two groups of friends in two separate cars set out together to attend a wedding, which is to take place at a church which is a hundred miles away. They estimate that at a steady speed of sixty-seven miles per hour, the journey should take just an hour and a half to get them there in good time for the ceremony. So the driver of car $A$ drives carefully at that steady speed all the way without interruption, to arrive at the church in time for the start of the service.

    Meanwhile the driver of the newer and faster car, $B$, aiming to arrive at the church at the same time as car $A$, dawdles in the slow lane of the motorway for the first fifty miles at a speed of fifty miles per hour, intending to make up time by driving faster over the next fifty-mile stage, so as to arrive in the same overall time of an hour and a half. However, at his slower speed of fifty miles per hour over the first part of the journey he has taken an hour to reach that halfway point, leaving him a half an hour to complete the next fifty miles. For this he would have to travel at a hundred miles per hour for a full half hour. This is thirty miles per hour over the official speed-limit. Sustaining this speed for a full half-hour is certain to get him a ‘ticket’ for speeding. So, having to stay within the limit he must keep his steady speed to the regulation seventy miles per hour. He and his passengers are dismayed, therefore, to find that when they arrive they are thirteen minutes late for the ceremony. This is another case of the ‘hare’ (car $B$) being beaten by the ‘tortoise’(car $A$) by thirteen minutes.

    This example illustrates the fact that time lost cannot be regained, a principle familiar to airline pilots, sea captains and racing drivers, namely, that if a speed is constrained, then a steady speed all the way makes for the shortest time, as opposed to a speed which varies over parts of the course. Another way of stating this same principle is to say that whatever has happened cannot be caused to unhappen.

    The inexorable nature of time is represented by the ‘time-rope’ in the Figs. 1 and 2, above, where each strand of the rope is the history of some object as viewed by the observer at the interactive now of the frame (i.e., the centre of the sphere, in Fig. 1). The objective history of the world, in these relativistic, or observational, terms is therefore the whole ‘net’, or network of objects and their developing histories that is familiar to us as objective, or Cartesian space, the grid-space of the surveyor and draughtsman – and, of course, ordinary commonsense. This objective, or communal space is as illustrated in Fig. 2. As the ‘hare and tortoise’ example shows, these individual time-lines, or histories, of objects do not progress everywhere in parallel. For instance, the history, or time-track, of car $A$ over the hundred mile course is obviously shorter than that of car $B$ over the same course. It is this same principle of the ‘staggering’ of the various ‘nows’ of the different objects in communal observer-space that applies in what is known as relativistic time dilation.

    7.3. Relativistic time dilation, Machian style

    Now it is a fact, which both Römer and Michelson ascertained (as discussed in Chapter 3, section 3.1) that in all observational dimensions shown in Figs. 1 and 2, the distances in metres are related to seconds of time in the ratio of conventional units, c, of 300,000 kilometres to the second. This means that every 300,000 kilometres of distance a body travels in space is equivalent to one second of time. The effect of this motion is easily demonstrated in the following way.

    Take an ordinary ruler and draw a line horizontally, left to right across the bottom of the page, as in Fig. 3 below. Then, from the start on the left of that line, draw another line, vertically up the left-hand side of the page, as shown in the figure. Next, with the ruler, mark off along these lines units of, say, centimetres. Label the horizontal line ‘time’, and the vertical one ‘distance-time’. Next, on the horizontal line (the ‘time-axis’) mark off each of these centimetres as ‘one second’. Then, mark off every centimetre up the vertical line as ‘300,000,000 metres’. You now have all it takes to calculate what Einstein’s Special Theory of Relativity calls time dilation.

    All one has to do, then, is to measure with the ruler the length in centimetres of the diagonal line $t_{R}$ in the figure. This is the geometrical (geometro-temporal) resultant, in seconds, of the two rectangular measures s/c and t. On this scale, s, in centimetres, on the vertical line, is the distance in units of 300,000,000 metres to the centimetre travelled by the body relative to the observer, and t is the time taken, on the same scale of one centimetre to the second, to cover that distance as registered by the clocks of the body itself (the so-called proper time). Note that all these measures are as viewed by the same observer. For instance, in watching the motion, the observer – in his telescope, say – sees the time registered by the travelling body in its own time t to travel the distance s. As he does so, he records that same motion as that same distance s travelled in the time $t_{R}$ ticked by his own observatory clocks (for ‘clocks’, here, read atoms and for their ‘ticks’ read spectral periods).

    Yes, it's as simple as that [78]. It bypasses all the mathematics that is usually considered obligatory for understanding Relativity. The only mathematics used here to derive the time dilation formula of Special Relativity is that of ordinary school-level geometry. This circumvents all the historical rigmarole about how light travels with respect to the vacuum, the ‘æther’ and so on. It also avoids all that tortuous historical and mathematical palaver that students of Relativity are obliged to undergo in having to tread in the footsteps of the Greats rather than be allowed to use their own plain common sense as in the construction of the above Fig. 3. However, if a more sophisticated, more academically acceptable mathematical representation is required, then that, also, is extremely simple – this just uses ordinary school-level algebra. Explicitly, anyone familiar with Pythagoras’ Theorem has all that is needed to derive, in this elementary way, precisely the same formula for time dilation as Einstein’s (see Appendix 1).

    It seems, then, that if, by chance, Pythagoras had extended his Theorem from dealing with just the three spatial dimensions, to include time as a fourth dimension (all measured in the same units) then he could, in theory, have anticipated Einstein by all of three thousand years.

    It is to be emphasised that this perhaps alarmingly simple result is obtained without involving any of the historical theories of ‘electromagnetism’, ‘light-signals’, the ‘æther’ and so on; hence it is in perfect accordance with the need for economy of conception called Ockham’s Razor (see Chapter 1). This, it has to be stressed, is a simplifying, not a simplistic, ‘dumbing-down’. It points out the plain fact that Pythagoras’s Theorem together with c as a conversion factor gives a simple and straightforward derivation of time dilation, upon which many facets of Einstein’s Special Theory are based. Many of the results of positive value for physics that follow from Einstein’s Special Theory follow from this extremely simple Pythagorean formulation. However, dispensing with all those bogus theoretical spin-offs of Einstein’s assumption, in his second axiom of Special Relativity, that light has a velocity in vacuo, makes all the jargon connected with that ‘light-in-space’

    assumption completely redundant. Strange to relate, in all of sixty years there has been no official recognition of this simple no-light-velocity deduction of time dilation in Relativity since it was first presented to the authorities in 1954. Where it has not been met with complete bemusement and apathy, it has, strangely enough, been met with hostility. The half century record of this saga of baffling hot and cold reception can be read in 'Philosophical Glimpses', a collection of papers and correspondence held at the Archives Department of the Civic Centre, Swansea, UK [79].

    7.4. The 'Twins Non-Paradox'

    Very soon after the publication of Special Relativity, it was thought by some physicists to contain a paradox connected with its central conception of the time dilation of bodies in relative motion. This stems from the mistaken assumption that when a pair of twins travel away from each other in space and then return together, the effect of the time dilation on each of the twins is the same for both of them (i.e., symmetrical) so that each one's duration is time-dilated equally relative to the other. This creates the conundrum that the twins end-up both younger and older than each other which is, of course, nonsense. It is as fallacious as saying that because the hare and the tortoise – or the new car and the old car in the above example – travel the same distance at the same average speed, they are bound to arrive at their destination at the same time. As we have seen, the splitting of the journey by the one car into two parts breaks the symmetry, so there is no such conundrum. This may be seen as follows.

    Let $t_{\mathrm{A}}$ be an atomic period or set of atomic periods (cell-divisions, birthdays or whatever) in the life of an observer $A$, and let $t_{\mathrm{B}}$ be that same set of periods in the life of another observer $B$. Now let these observers be a pair of twins starting close together in ideally empty Euclidean space and then moving apart at a uniform speed $v$ relative to each other. Let us also assume that throughout the motion they keep observing each other through telescopes fitted with spectroscopes which record the number of atomic periods counted. With $v$ in the standard time dilation formula (see Appendix 1) being the same for both twins, the time dilation of each twin relative to the other is, so far, perfectly symmetrical.

    But now let us suppose that as viewed in $B$'s spectroscope, twin $A$, having travelled an observational distance $s$ in the time $t_{\mathrm{A}}$ registered by $A$'s own atomic clocks, immediately reverses his motion to return to $B$ over that same observational distance s at the same speed v as that with which he set out. As Einstein pointed out in his reply to Pope's query on this very subject, this means that one or the other of the twins, in this case, A, has to reverse his velocity. This reversal, for A, is accompanied by all the visceral effects that are typically felt by anyone in, say, a bus or a car when the driver accelerates or applies his brakes. These visceral effects are not, of course, felt by observer B who witnesses that reversal from afar. In other words, the true reversal occurs immediately in A's reference-frame but not B's. In that case, the observations of the same motion and of the same turnaround are by no means symmetrical.

    Now it is well known that when the motion of a body seen in a telescope changes from that of recession to one of approach, that change is immediately accompanied by a shift in its spectrum from red (longer) to blue (shorter) periods. Thus, as seen in B's telescope, when A reverses his motion relative to B, that reversal is signified to B by a change in A's spectral periods from red to blue in the usual way. This reversal of A's motion, however, which, of course, takes place right on the spot for A, is not the same for B. For B that event takes place, as we say, remotely, at a distance-time s/c away. Meanwhile, from A's point of view, just as soon as he reverses his motion, the shift from red to blue in his spectroscopic observation of B switches immediately from red to blue, not remotely, as in B's spectroscopic observation of him. In other words, that spectral shift from red to blue in each twin's observation of the other is not symmetrical, as is so often falsely assumed by the 'Dissidents'. It is unarguably asymmetrical [79].

    So the upshot of this is that the number of A's atomic periods counted by B in his spectroscope up till the moment of the reversal, cannot be the same as B's periods counted by A in his spectroscope. This is because spectroscope A counts the atomic periods of B from the start of the motion right up till the moment of A's reversing action, whereas spectroscope B counts the atomic periods of A up till the point of him seeing it remotely at the distance s, which means that B registers red-shifted periods of A for longer than A registers red-shifted periods of B, hence, conversely, A registers a smaller proportion of blue-shifted to red-shifted periods of B than B registers of A.

    Plainly then, there is no symmetry, hence no paradox. If it is A who takes that reversing action, then it is A who ends up the younger (in terms of his atomic periods, cell-division rates, etc.) On the other hand, if it is B who takes that action, then the situation is the opposite way around. This is another instance of the ‘hare and tortoise’ principle, discussed above, which asserts that time lost can never be regained. The ‘hare’ in this case is twin $A$, who breaks his journey half way, with the result that when he reaches the destination his atomic clocks – hence his heart-beats, cell-division rates, birthdays, etc. – are found to be lagging behind those of twin $B$, whose clocks, ticking at a steady rate throughout, end-up ahead of $A$’s $^{[80]}$. This is illustrated in the conception of ‘time-slides’ included by Osborne in his monograph The Nature of Time $^{[81]}$.

    Undoubtedly, the strangest response to this demonstration is that although this time dilation has to be accepted, it applies ‘only to clocks’ and cannot affect people in any way – which is, surely, nonsense, since there is no way in which time dilation can affect an observer’s ‘clocks’ without time-dilating all other time processes in that observer frame, not excluding the life processes of the observer himself $^{[82]}$. This makes it pertinent to mention, at this point, the intellectual challenge that this piece of rather obvious – albeit rather intricate – piece of commonsense reasoning presents to some people, for instance, those of the Dingle-ite persuasion described in Chapter 2, section 2. This has led to a burgeoning of Internet forums dedicated to what can only be labelled as ‘Einstein Bashing’. This is by people whose sole aim in life seems to be, under the name of ‘Dissidence’, as rudely and aggressively as possible to attack anything that supports the Theory of Relativity. Even when presented with copies of Einstein’s letter they spurn it with no sign whatsoever of wanting to understand what Einstein was saying. Perhaps the most charitable thing that can be said about these people is that they are, unwittingly, part of the same sort of sociological phenomenon of mob-rule that once manifested itself in witch-burnings, lynchings, beheadings and the like, that were meted out to anyone threatening the composure of their settled beliefs. ‘Einstein’ and ‘Relativity’ pose that kind of threat to the long-established, conceptually ingrained classical view of time and space as a self-existing, self-extended and self-enduring nothingness, or void, in which time advances uniformly, everywhere in parallel, in the vacuum as in matter. This, they imagine, is not as we ever see it but only as ‘God sees it’, continuously behind all our pathetically inadequate attempts to measure it with our clocks and other measuring devices. So, for these people it seems that in denying the existence of this ‘God’s-eye-view’ of space and time we are denying the very existence of God himself, which fills them, albeit subliminally, with the same sort of superstitious, and incipiently religious dread as that with which, as history attests, just about every truly radical paradigm shift in thinking was attended. How many more centuries, one wonders, must this present stubbornness persist against abandoning that presumptuous ‘God’s-eye-view’ of things and grasping the obvious truth of relativism?

    7.5. Quantised spectra

    As already discussed, the difference between Einstein’s Relativity and that of his relativistic forerunner, Mach, is that whereas Einstein’s Relativity is that of a continuum or plenum, Mach’s is a discontinuum or discretum. That is to say, it is basically quantised in informational packages which he called ‘sense-data’. Also discussed was the identification, in Neo-Machian Normal Realism, of these data with the light-quantum of Planck and Einstein – Einstein’s so-called ‘photons’. (Recall that in Normal Realism there are no such things as ‘photons’, but only ‘photo’ (plural of ‘photum’), meaning non-travelling quanta of light-energy or ‘quantum pixels’.)

    In the last section, we discussed the bar-code-like frequency-lines of the light-spectrum. These are the basic data, the quantum pixels of the Neo-Machian discretum. The regularities of these lines were first formulated by Joseph Balmer, who experimented with various formulations until, eventually, he found one to fit. This is the well-known formula for the series of frequencies of basic hydrogen (see Appendix 2). Balmer offered no explanation for these regularities, which he discovered purely by trial and error. An explanation was offered, eventually, by Niels Bohr, that was based on the electrodynamic theory prevalent at the time. However, Normal Realism by-passes that whole theoretical tradition of ‘electrodynamical’ explanations which is based on the redundant and nonsensical ‘light-velocity’ interpretation of c. This means that Normal Realism needs to offer an explanation of its own for those spectral regularities.

    So far, the Normal Realist explanation of phenomena such as time dilation, etc., has been couched in terms of pure logic, with little or no dependence on conventional historical precepts. With the greatest respect for those heroes of the illustrious twentieth century, the creators of the present edifice of Scientific Theory, for the sake of the present exercise we lay aside those conventional explanations and see whether the pure logic that has taken us thus far may take us even further. For instance, the same formula that was discovered by Balmer and Rydberg by the method of pure trial and error, and explained by Bohr in terms of traditional electrodynamics, can be derived much more directly from the relativistic time formula by pure syllogistic logic (that is, simply by substituting terms from one formula into another, as demonstrated in Appendix 3).

    In this way, the formula for quantised spectra is deduced directly from the relativistic time formula, bypassing, with respect, as we say, the whole history of the electrodynamics of Niels Bohr, et al. Again, this is with c interpreted as a pure constant expressing the ratio of conventional measures of metres to seconds instead of the traditional ‘speed of light’. Needless to say, the usual postulates of space-travelling ‘waves’ – ‘electromagnetic’ or ‘æthereal’ – ‘photons’ or whatever, take no part in these deductions. Dispensing with so much conceptual encumbrance should be as advantageous for the future of physical science and philosophy as, undoubtedly, it would be disturbing for current convention. This is almost certainly why even those authorities who have verified the mathematics of this demonstration, have ignored its philosophical significance. For instance, in the Citation Index (circa 1987), the paper in which this ‘New Approach’ to the time dilation formula of Special Relativity appeared it was described simply as ‘a modelling approach to Relativity’[83]. The fact that it did not involve the concept of ‘light-velocity’ was completely ignored. Others, like Bondi, saw it as no more than a handy ‘teaching aid’ for teaching students about standard Relativity, leaving it to Pope, as he (Bondi) said, to develop any philosophical significance it might have for the conduct of physics and the Philosophy of Science, generally.

    On this subject of ‘teaching aids’ perhaps the following remarks will be useful in thinking of quanta in the Normal Realist context. These quanta or phota are, as it were, the coinage of all energy-transactions between physical objects. They have differences in value, like the penny, the five-pence and pound coins in British money, or the nickels and dimes of US currency. These coins cannot be cut into pieces which have any monetary value. Nor is there any significant question of how they are transported, that is, how they travel, say, between one purse or pocket and another. They may be stored or used, but unlike the coins in our analogy, the real quanta cannot be counterfeited. Nor can they be created or destroyed, which would be disallowed by the law of action conservation. In short, they are truly atomic in the full sense of that word as coined by the original Greek atomists. In our next chapter we logically project the further Physical and Philosophical implications of these conceptions.

    CHAPTER 8

    The Normal Realist Angular Momentum Synthesis

    In the previous chapters the traditional conception of light as a process taking place in vacuo was subjected to a critical logical examination, the result of which has been to determine that this conception is not only unempirical but is also completely fanciful. Fortunately, however, it transpires that this demonstrably fallacious, space-travelling interpretation of light is surplus to requirements in our understanding of the true nature of light in relation to both Relativity and Quantum Theory. But, of course, in that traditional, mechanistic approach to physical phenomena, light is not the only inscrutable agency acting in vacuo. Another is what is called ‘gravitation’, in which ‘gravity’ is presented as an invisible power pulling bodies together in space with a strength proportional to their combined masses. Quite evidently the logical arguments which apply to in vacuo light-conduction apply also to this traditional concept of ‘gravity’ as an invisible, in vacuo ‘force’.

    8.1. Newton’s in vacuo ‘gravitational force’.

    What is customarily called ‘gravity’ is a natural phenomenon, known to everyone as an invisible force with which the Earth presses upwards against everything on its surface. ‘Whoa, there! What’s that again? ‘Upwards’? Surely, this should be downwards! For centuries, ‘gravity’ has been commonly understood as a force which presses downwards on things, which makes them fall and weigh what they do. However, it is well known, by skydivers, astronauts and the like, that in a state of freefall, no such downward force can be felt. This attests to the fact that in such circumstances (freefall) there is no such thing manifest as ‘gravity’; that in reality, what is called ‘gravity’ is an upward force which the surfaces of planets and other massive objects exert on bodies preventing them from following their natural force-free trajectories which, unless they have sufficient angular momentum to orbit freely above the Earth’s surface, they would follow, close to the Earth’s centre, down below our feet.

    The plain fact, then, is that in space there is no such thing as ‘gravity’. A prime example of an un-empirical theory, therefore, is Newton’s theory of gravitation as an invisible force acting in space (in vacuo). This unempirical character of Newtonian ‘gravitation’ was realised by Einstein, which is why he sought to replace that theory with one of his own. This was his theory of a space-time substratum in which the mass of a body ‘warps’ what would otherwise be a Euclidean continuum, like knots in the grain of wood causing freely moving bodies to move in the way that is observed. Unfortunately for this Einsteinian ‘space-time continuum’, it is every bit as inscrutable as the ‘gravitational field’ of Newton which Einstein sought to replace. Not only that but this continuum of Einstein’s stands diametrically opposed to the discretum demanded by quantum physics and by his relativistic precursor, Mach.

    Both Newton’s and Einstein’s theories are therefore over-elaborations of the bare facts concerning the phenomenon of motion; of orbital motion in particular. For instance, in Newton’s theory there are two ‘Laws’ which exactly cancel and thereby annul each other. One Law states that all bodies with no forces acting upon them either remain stationary or travel in straight lines. Then the other law, his ‘Law of Universal Gravitation’ rules that no bodies ever behave in the way his First Law demands because they are prevented from doing so by an assumed ‘universal gravitational force’. In this way, a body in free orbital motion is supposed to be held in suspension between two equal and opposite ‘forces’, one being the centre-seeking force of ‘gravitation’ drawing the bodies together, and the other being the ‘centrifugal’ force seeking to pull them out of their ‘gravitational’ orbits and allowing them to fly off into their allegedly ‘rectilinear’ trajectories.

    Quite plainly, then, this theory of Newton’s does not follow the principle of Ockham’s Razor discussed in the previous chapters. Just as in the case of ‘light-velocity’, this alleged in vacuo ‘gravitation’ of Newton’s raises unnecessary and chronically enervating questions as to how this fictitious ‘force’ is conducted and at what speed. Is it by travelling particles (‘gravitons’) or by waves? This hoary question has remained unanswered to the present day. Despite huge amounts of money, time and energy spent on trying to find evidence for the existence of these fictitious entities with a variety of the most expensive apparatuses, not a single trace of them has ever emerged. This lends point to what was said in Chapter 1 about not only the conceptual extravagance but also the financial extravagance of pursuing over-intellectualised theories.

    There will be more discussion on this particular point in due course. In the meantime, let us examine the phenomenon of free orbital motion in a more empirical, more conceptually economical light.

    8.2. Orbital angular momentum

    Like Newton, Aristotle was interested in the phenomenon of orbital motion. In contrast to Newton, however, he eschewed the idea of force-free motion as fundamentally rectilinear. He argued that force-free uninterrupted straight-line motion is impossible. This, he reasoned, is because motion in a straight line has to begin and end somewhere, hence is interrupted (subject to a force) or else it continues forever, which is unthinkable. The only truly uninterrupted (force-free) motion, therefore, Aristotle concluded, has to be cyclic or orbital, which, unlike Newton’s First Law, corresponds directly with observation.

    Following Aristotle, then, the only force-free motion we may contemplate in Normal Realism is orbital motion. Such motion has an associated orbital angular momentum about a central point of reference. Bodies in an angular momentum relation are automatically paired and balanced without need of any ‘force’, whether ‘centripetal’ (e.g., ‘gravitational’) or ‘centrifugal’. The motions of bodies in this relation are correlated in such a way as to orbit one another, like the Moon around the Earth and the planets around the Sun, all in perfect correlation, with there being no question as to how they follow those trajectories or how they influence one another at a distance. Sufficient to say that in space everything orbits everything else – at least, unless forced to do otherwise. This is where force – real force – comes into play, as that which, in one way or another, prevents bodies from following their natural force-free orbits.

    Angular momentum therefore goes hand-in-hand with what is called ‘action-at-a-distance’, which is no more than a reciprocal balance between bodies in accordance with the Law of Moments. In this case, there is no question of the ‘speed’ of this influence, as in the imaginary ‘speed of gravity’. The correlation of orbital motions is automatic. In this way of thinking, the universe is a whole nexus of angular momentum relations. This holds everything together, in the same manner as Newton’s ‘gravity’, but with no need of any mysterious in vacuo ‘gravitational force’. Angular momentum proves to be sufficient in itself to explain the orbital motions of bodies in purely observational terms without any theoretical elaboration (see Appendix 4). As stated earlier, this approach has become known as the Pope-Osborne Angular Momentum Synthesis (POAMS). More details on this subject are available in the books and papers listed on the POAMS website [84].

    It is surely strange that this self-sufficiency of angular momentum to explain orbital motion without ‘gravitation’ is not generally realised. The formulae which demonstrate this, as shown in Appendix 4, are very much simplified. For instance there are, in fact, practically no ideally circular orbits in nature. Angular momentum trajectories of orbiting bodies are mostly elliptical, but they may be parabolic, hyperbolic, etc., hence unclosed, such as those of bullets and shells. However, to include all the mathematical formulae required for dealing with these situations would compromise the aim of this book, which is to provide, for the ordinary commonsense thinker, the simplest, most basic understanding of this Neo-Machian approach to physical phenomena.

    8.2.1. Newton’s falling apple

    Of course, this angular momentum explanation of force-free motion raises questions. One of these is bound to be that if there is no such thing as ‘gravity’, then what is it that makes things weigh what they do on the Earth’s surface? What made Newton’s apple fall, for instance, if there’s no such thing as gravity? These are the same questions as might be levelled at Einstein’s General Theory, according to which ‘gravity’ is equivalent to an upwards acceleration, so that the force of ‘gravity’ is the same as what we would feel in a rocket ship in space accelerating at a rate of 9.8 metres per second per second, pressing us to the floor of the vessel. This implies that our Earth is accelerating under our feet, which is, to say the least, counter-intuitive. POAMS avoids this conundrum. The answer it gives is that like all bodies on the Earth’s surface, the apple, where it hangs on the twig, does not have enough angular momentum imparted to it by the Earth’s rotation to orbit freely around the Earth, clear of the Earth’s surface. Without that sufficient amount of orbital angular momentum it should rest at or near the Earth’s centre. But since the twig prevents it from doing that, the apple pulls on the twig with the real reactionary force we call its weight. When the twig breaks, that force converts to a downwards acceleration of 9.8 metres per second per second until, once again, it is prevented from falling further by the ground – or by Newton’s head, as the case may be. Meanwhile, for all bodies resting on the ground the reactionary upwards force exerted by the Earth’s surface or a weighing-scale is 9.8 newtons for every kilogram of mass. This is not an accelerating force exerted on us as in General Relativity. It is simply a force with which the Earth’s surface presses against any body which prevents it from seeking the orbit it should have with respect to the Earth’s centre [85]. Gravitational effects are all explicable in terms of angular momentum, of one form or another.

    Another question is that if everything is connected with everything else in an overall angular momentum nexus, then doesn’t that entail another Eleatic stasis or cosmical gridlock? (See Chapter 2, section 2.1.2.) The answer is no. Like action, which is quantised in units of Planck’s constant, $h$, the magnitude of angular momentum is quantised in units of $h/2\pi$, called the ‘reduced Planck’s constant’ [86]. This means that at its most fundamental level, angular momentum is atomised and indeterminate, so that there is no question of its forming a monolithic continuum like the Eleatic One – or, of course, Einstein’s ‘space-time continuum’.

    A further inevitable question is that if everything is connected instantly with everything else in this angular momentum matrix, then why doesn’t everything happen everywhere in the universe all at once? The answer is that ‘instantaneity’, here, does not entail simultaneity, so although each quantum connection between bodies is instantaneous, these quantum instantaneities do not occur all together simultaneously. These connections are what logicians call intransitive. For example, to say that ‘Alan is the son of Bert’ and that ‘Bert is the son of Charles’ does not mean that ‘Alan is the son of Charles’ (Alan is, of course, the grandson of Charles). In the same way, to say that an atom $A$ is instantly quantum connected to atom $B$ and that atom $B$ is instantly quantum connected to atom $C$ does not mean that atom $A$ is instantly quantum connected to $C$. Hence, in the universal angular momentum nexus there is no overall simultaneity of connection, of the sort imagined by the Eleatics and classical physicists. Everything, at root, is random and indeterminate, and it is only on the macro-phenomenal level that there is anything like ‘determinacy’, which is never more than an approach towards maximum probability, away from complete randomness. (See Chapter 2, section 2.4.) In short, then, unlike the determinate monolithic continua of Parmenides and Einstein, the angular momentum nexus is indeterminate and essentially mobile. That is to say, in the overall angular momentum nexus there is nothing predetermined or pre-ordained. There is always room for actions which change the probabilities from those of maximum disorder towards minimum disorder (order), signifying the freedom for any agent to ‘change the world’ by deliberation or design. In other words, it does not in any way support the fatalism expressed in the works of writers such as the 16$^{th}$ century philosopher Thomas

    Hobbes, the 20$^{th}$ century psychologist B. F. Skinner (1904-1990) or the 11$^{th}$ century Persian polymath and poet, Omar Khayyam.

    8.3. The interactive present moment

    The question now arises as to where the ‘present moment’ in this angular momentum nexus is, since, as Relativity reveals, there is no universally simultaneous instant throughout the system. The only answer is that the present moment is that point in the time of any object or observer where it interacts or freely communicates with other observers/instruments in the world in general. These ‘points in time’ are, of course, different for different observers, due to their various cumulative time dilations with respect to one another, which means that each observer/agent in its own time-frame has its own designated time allocation for that interactive present moment. So each observational present moment is, as it were, a projected cross-section of the whole visible universe as it presents itself relative to that specific observer. As already discussed (Chapter 5, section 5.1), it is only in communication with others like oneself who project the ‘universe’ in that same or similar way, that the objectivity of that universe is assured. This dispels the presumed ubiquitous, ‘God’s-eye-view’ of the universe adopted by classical Physics and Cosmology – and General Relativity – in favour of the observationist, or relativist philosophy of physics espoused by Mach.

    There is, then, no universally extended present moment, or ‘cutting-edge’ to time. Time evolves at points only, of the sort shown in Fig. 1, Chapter 7, and every point has its own developmental present moment, independently of all the rest. The ‘edge of time’ advances, therefore, so to speak, not like the cutting-edge of a cosmically extended knife-blade. It is more like a phalanx of thrusting spears than a cosmic guillotine. Within that temporal phalanx of ‘spear-thrusters’, as it were, there is an advancing ‘front’ in which the individuals are in communication with one another, individually and independently but with a common purpose. This, by analogy, is the world of natural phenomena with which we are normally familiar. It is the world of physical reality in which are communally projected the three Cartesian dimensions of objective frame-space, evolving all over in a single, or global, arbitrarily chosen time-reference such as, for instance, standard Greenwich Mean Time (GMT). This is the world that is commonly described in terms of our traditional textbooks of physics. It is the world in which we observe and describe the motions of bodies, such as the orbital motions of planets, satellites, stars in spiral galaxies and so on, as though observed, if at all, by an indescribable ubiquitous presence (demon or deity). The fact, then, that in these pages we describe physical phenomena such as the orbital angular momenta of bodies in space, of our Earth around the Sun, of artificial satellites in terrestrial or solar orbit, ‘electrons’ around ‘nuclei’, etc., etc., as though without any reference to specified central points of perspective (‘observers’ in conventional relativistic terms) by no means implies an abandonment of relativism in favour of classical absolutism. It is the seminal claim of Normal Realism that these two different perspectives of physical reality are perfectly complementary, hence can exist happily together – so long, of course, as the speeds of motion traditionally described do not far exceed those of ordinary objects such as planes, bullets, rockets, and the like, in which case, relativity necessarily comes into play with appropriate changes in the use of language.

    So saying, and bearing this caveat in mind, we now continue to examine the phenomenon of orbital motion in its Cartesian, or frame-space (textbook-style) aspect.

    8.4. Orbital time dilation

    Now in the same way that the Balmer-Rydberg formula for atomic spectra can be deduced from the relativistic time-formula (Appendix 3), so can the formula for orbital time dilation, as witnessed in the Global Positioning Satellites (GPS) and in situations of ‘strong gravity’. This is explained in Appendix 4 which shows how, again syllogistically, simply substituting the term $GM/r$ for $\nu^2$ in the time dilation formula expresses that formula in terms of potential energy. Unlike the classical ‘gravitational potential’ of Newton, the sign of this potential energy is positive. That is to say, the zero of reference for that angular momentum potential, hence the objective zero on the scale of time dilation, lies at the greatest distance from the presence of any massive body. Explicitly, for the orbital radius, $r$, theoretically infinite in the orbital time dilation equation, the orbital speed $\nu = 0$ and so the time dilation is zero. Then, for instance, the nearer an atomic or any other kind of clock approaches, say, the Earth, the slower it runs. From this POAMS point of view, therefore, there is a predictable time dilation, hence a slowing of clocks in satellites as they orbit closer to the Earth, the Sun, or some other massive body, and a quickening of those clocks as they approach the farthest distance from any such bodies. In Normal Realism this ideal datum-zero on the scale of time dilations is called Deep Space Time (DST). It is a cosmic time-standard analogous to our terrestrial GMT but

    ‘universal’ in the sense of meaning in which GMT is not. That is to say, it is ‘universal’ in the same sense that the datum zero of the temperature scale is ‘universal’ without this implying that ‘the universe itself’ is at zero degrees Kelvin. Nevertheless, this Deep Space Time (DST) does, in a way, provide a ‘preferred frame’ of reference for the measuring of motion and time dilation, insofar as it is the time of a body which, theoretically, has no motion in itself but with reference to which all other bodies are either moving or stationary.

    As it stands this simple formula for orbital time dilation applies not only to orbits but also to straight up-and-down motion of a body with respect to the central mass $M$. This latter is confirmed by the Pound-Rebka experiment (see below) [87]. This slowing of clocks is verified by engineers and scientists involved with satellite systems such as those of the American Global Positioning System (GPS) and its Russian equivalent, GLONASS. Discussions with these experts have disclosed that clocks on these satellites run quicker than clocks on the Earth’s surface, albeit slower than clocks further out in space, which is direct confirmation of our POAMS prediction.

    In 1960, Robert V. Pound and Glen A. Rebka carried out an experiment employing Mössbauer (i.e., gamma) emitters. In using these recoil-less nuclear emitters and absorbers, the experimenters discovered that in order to resonate with an emitter of standard gamma radiation higher up in the lift-shaft of a tall building, an observing instrument (gamma absorber) placed lower down in the shaft had to be tuned to absorb blue-shifted periods. That is to say, it had to be run more quickly than the emitter above. On the other hand, in order to resonate with an emitter lower down, the observing instrument had to be tuned to receive red-shifted periods. That is, it had to be run more slowly than normal. From the point of view of the observing instrument, therefore, those nuclear clocks that were higher up the shaft manifested periods shorter than the standard period (their frequency was blued, or quickened) and those below manifested periods that were longer (their frequency was reddened, or slowed). In other words, if the Pound-Rebka results are correct, then the higher in a tower a nuclear clock is raised, the faster it ticks, and the lower it descends, the slower it ticks – the standard tick being, in this case, a single cycle of the gamma quantum. This experiment by Pound and Rebka is usually taken as one confirmation of Einstein’s prediction, in his General Relativity, that clocks in a gravitational field are slowed in proportion to the strength of the field. (As the reader will no doubt have realised, our formula predicts this same phenomenon without any of the conventional presuppositions of ‘gravitational fields’.)

    It follows from the orbital time dilation formula that the Moon in terrestrial orbit loses just over 498 nanoseconds DST every 24 hours – as would any clock orbiting at that distance from Earth. Relative to DST, terrestrial clocks in solar orbit lose 426,160 nanoseconds every 24 hours [88]. From the average data supplied by the GPS engineers, the formula predicts for a satellite in circular orbit above the Earth’s surface, a loss of 7216 nanoseconds per 24 hours DST. By that same formula, a clock orbiting a mass equal to that of our Earth at a distance equivalent to that of the Earth’s radius would lose 30075 nanoseconds per 24 hours DST. But of course, the clocks that register standard Earth time (GMT) are not orbiting freely at an Earth-orbiting, surface-skimming velocity of 7,910 metres per second. Being Earth-bound, the fastest those clocks may travel is at the Earth’s smaller equatorial spin-speed of 464.74 metres per second. Moreover, $G$ in our formula has its standard value only for bodies in free orbit. Nevertheless, taking the free-orbiting time-standard as approximate to GMT, we have the result that, as measured from Earth, the satellite gains 22859 nanoseconds per day, which is approximately 60% of the reported average value of 38700 nanoseconds gained by those satellites as measured from the ground. However, the values predicted by the formula, while qualitatively correct, are deduced for ideally circular orbits involving no more than just two bodies in a single instance whereas, in reality, all the bodies of the solar system and beyond are part of the same overall angular momentum system. For instance, while the satellite is orbiting the Earth, both bodies are in orbital relations with the Sun, the Moon and, incipiently, all the other planets and satellites of the solar system, which presents us with the notoriously difficult-to-solve ‘Many Body Problem’. In view of this exigency we would hardly expect our simple formula to be one hundred percent accurate. Indeed, it would be highly suspicious if it were.

    To summarise this section: in this simple and straightforward way we move directly from a time dilation formula for rectilinear motion as in Special Relativity, to a time dilation formula for orbital motion comparable with that of General Relativity. However, as can be seen, this is without any reference whatsoever to the usual theoretical rationale of ‘gravitation’, ‘photons’, etc., which language, from the Normal Realist point of view, and in accordance with Ockham’s Razor, may therefore be regarded as redundant.

    8.5. Educational lets and hindrances in the inculcation of new ideas

    For the purposes of standard Education, the study of Physics is always, for the most part, a familiarisation with the meandering exploratory tracks laid by those staunch pioneers of the subject who, in their day, set the standard for students to follow. In this way, the logic of the subject, as studied at university, is all too often confused with its circumstantial history. In their studies, students are encouraged not so much to think for themselves, ‘outside the box’, so to speak, as to think along those historical lines laid down by the Greats. Any ahistorical, off-the-track lateral thinking has to be done only when the student shows proficiency in the subject into which he has been educationally conditioned. Any pristine idea he might have of his own, faced with nature direct, if it has to be to be presented for authoritative consideration, has to be judged for its conformity with the standards of formal Education. On this basis, students are examined, not for truly original thought, but almost entirely on familiarity with the precepts formulated by the custodians of the System.

    Now this kind of educational proficiency is all very well for ordinary social purposes. Any society must have standards strict enough to avoid anarchy. On the other hand, in exploratory subjects like Science and Philosophy, these standards, if too strictly adhered to, instead of encouraging progress in thinking, may actually inhibit it, or even ‘straitjacket’ it altogether. In this way, the prospect of any natural, truly logical, ahistorical advancement in understanding may well be lost.

    This, unfortunately, is what can happen to man’s natural philosophical and scientific curiosity in those societies which have become educationally predominant, hence where the need for educational continuity and social integrity are paramount. It is of course, what happened to our Western society during its formative, medieval period, when Education, under the stewardship of Catholic Christianity ruled all attempts at thinking with a ‘big stick’. Although some of the central ideas of that system have been overturned – by people such as Copernicus and Galileo, who went in fear of their lives for it – the System itself has survived more or less intact, whereby scholarship remains at a premium and the prospect of revolution as much a threat to stability as ever. Set in that pattern of scholasticism, we may miss out altogether on the possibility that our labyrinthine tradition of scientific philosophy may be logically by-passed or short-circuited by completely new thinking.

    However, in the same way that modern highways may be built to circumvent all the old lanes and byways laid down by horses and carts, we should now, surely, be leaving those old, beloved routes to be explored by hikers and tourists. In other words, we ought now to be building new and more efficient routes for the fast transport of ideas, leaving those ancient and circuitous thought-systems to be studied by students of that illustrious history.

    Something needs to be said here about the situation witnessed in Higher Education at this time of writing, where it is perfectly obvious to any uncommitted objective observer that universities are not what they were once were. In their best days these institutions were dedicated mainly to fostering free and unfettered, open-ended research. Now they have become, in some ways, more the servants of Commerce and Industry than institutes for the pursuit of natural philosophy. University tutors who would once have had to spend no more than 10% of their time on finance and administration and 90% on research, have in some cases been instructed to reverse those figures towards spending 90% of their time on affairs of finance and administration and no more than 10% on research [89]. Entrance qualifications are aimed more towards enticing fee-paying students to take up courses in subjects far more fitted for making contributions to the national and global economy than more traditional subjects. In all of this, it is the money markets which, implicitly, ‘call the shots’. Physics, like Philosophy before it, is practically consigned to Educational oblivion, along with subjects under the heading of the M.A. (‘Master of Arts’). This, of course, is in favour of more ‘cost-effective’, end-assured and commercially viable subjects like Business Studies, Media Studies and Computing.

    No longer is there any encouragement for researchers freely to explore entirely open-ended and serendipitous ‘blue-sky’ lines of investigation. Tenure for such dedicated truth-chasers to ‘follow their star’ in the peace and security of an academic provision is a thing of the past. Everything has to be ‘cost-effective’ and ‘risk-assessed’. As someone has jocularly remarked, for Columbus to discover America, nowadays he would first have to be fully risk-assessed and show his sponsors a map of the United States before he was allowed to set sail.

    Indeed, it is fair to say, that right now, money, love of which is the proverbial ‘root of all evil’, has become an all-obscuring thicket in the path of true scientific and philosophical progress. All that is left to extant Education is to serve the common concerns of institutions social, political and financial. Nowadays, many universities are clearly no more than glorified technical colleges and business colleges, with natural philosophy nowhere to be seen, except vestigially in the degree ironically called the ‘Doctor of Philosophy’, now awarded for just about anything except Philosophy.

    In the following chapters, we make further suggestions as to how, now that our traditions of Physics and Cosmology have so plainly lost their way in pursuit of anything but natural truth and reality, the radical method of Physical-cum-Philosophical advance suggested in this book may be put into effect in addressing the need for a cleaned-up ‘New Physics’ as anticipated by Anderson, et al.

    CHAPTER 9

    Democritus Inside-Out

    The previous chapter ended with a short discussion of the pressing need for a new resurgent direction in higher education. This is to recover what used to be the aim of universities, to provide for and encourage the exercise of free-range intelligent curiosity in philosophically-minded students. This is the reason why much of the research which has led to the writing of this book has had to be carried out extramurally. The advantage of this is that there is no price-tag on any preconceived route to be taken by the thoughts, which are therefore left, unhindered, to choose their own purely logical direction. The result of this, so far, has been to discover how, when stripped of their educational overlay of traditional precept and associated jargon, a new physics is possible in which relativity and the quantum are seen to be no more nor less than perfectly complementary aspects of the same physical phenomena. In this next section we emphasise the new departure of physics from Democritan materialism towards a consistently relativistic, energy-based view of matter.

    9.1. ‘Wheels within wheels’: the angular momentum universe

    From what has been said so far about light and phenomena in the context of Relativity, it should be plain that the customary materialist conception of ‘brute matter’ as something absolutely substantial and self-sufficient is an illusion. One has only to think about it on the commonsense level to realise that this is so. For instance, consider a bicycle wheel with all the spokes removed except for just so many as may be sufficient to keep the wheel rigid and rotating in a plane. With that wheel stationary, it is easy enough to throw, say, a bead or a small ball-bearing through the wheel without hitting the spokes. But now set the wheel spinning. Obviously, the chances of the projectile penetrating the wheel are reduced. By that same token, the wheel may be spun so fast – assuming, of course, that it doesn’t disintegrate – that it is impossible for anything at any normal throwing rate to penetrate it. The wheel is now more like a solid disc than the original spoked wheel.

    What, then, has been added to the wheel to create that solidity? Not matter, certainly, but kinetic energy. It is this energy which makes the wheel impenetrable, or solid. On the other hand, with the wheel spinning in this way, as the throwing-speed of the projectile is increased, its chances of penetrating the wheel are larger. This is why a nail-gun can fire a nail into concrete, because the faster the nail travels the more the speed of its molecules matches those of the concrete, enabling the molecules of the nail and of the concrete to mingle and fuse together at the point of contact.

    This may give some clue as to how matter and energy are interchangeable. The energy imparted to the wheel to set it spinning is stored in the form of solidity but is recoverable in any effort to brake the motion or slow it down, as anyone knows who has attempted to stop a fast spinning bicycle wheel by hand. In that same way, the energy that is stored in substances such as oil, coal, etc., from ancient sunlight, is recoverable, just like the energy stored in the wheel, by some kind or another of downgrading transformation, such as combustion or whatever, in accordance with the Law of Entropy (see Chapter 5, section 5.3.1). The most dramatic instance of this interchangeability of mass and energy is, of course, that well-known relativistic formula,$e = mc^2$. In Normal Realism this is deduced directly and logically from the time dilation formula, as in Appendix 3. This formula implies that the energy$e$ imparted to an object in order to increase its velocity turns more or less completely into mass$m$, which means that the amount of energy applied to the body in order to accelerate it further increases according to the principle of diminishing returns. So, as the body approaches its 'speed limit'$c$, the amount of energy imparted to accelerate it turns more and more into mass, in that ratio of units$c^2$, towards an upper limit where, theoretically, the applied energy becomes all mass, which means that it cannot be accelerated further by any increase in applied energy. The more massive an atom, therefore, the more energy it contains. So great is this energy in some isotopes, such as those of uranium and plutonium, that the nuclei are unstable. This instability can produce the catastrophic atomic disintegration and release of the stored energy that was witnessed in the Japanese cities of Hiroshima and Nagasaki during World War II.

    Ultimately, then, in Normal Realism there is no such thing as mass, existing in and of itself. Mass is just one of the triad of inseparable fundamental measures, mass, length and time, in all forms of angular momentum. From the macro-scale to the micro-scale, there are just more and more 'wheels within wheels' of angular momentum, right down to the ultimately irreducible quantum of angular momentum,$h/2\pi$, which, as such, is not just a mass, although mass is part of its description. This means that there is no ultimate mass-particle but only ultimate 'wheels' – that is to say, only hierarchies of paired and balanced mass-particles orbiting in integral angular momentum relations around barycentres which, in themselves, are empty of matter. The Normal Realist ‘atom’ can be thought of as an indivisible angular momentum system in this way and is very different from the solid-all-through atom of Democritus. This will be discussed in due course.

    Now in some lectures and conferences on the subject of Normal Realism and POAMS, commentators have remarked that two theories have been presented: one about light and the other about angular momentum [90]. It is easy to see why this might seem to be the case, since there appears to be no obvious connection between light, as customarily conceived, and the phenomenon of angular momentum. There is, however, an intimate – indeed, inseparable – connection between light and angular momentum, which is explained as follows.

    As we have seen, light is action in units of the product of energy and time in irreducible units of Planck’s constant, h. From this it is plain that the light-quantum is not simply a mass in kilograms. A common mistake, to be studiously avoided at all costs, therefore, is to think of this quantum as a ‘particle’, like the atom of Democritus. In those Democritan terms, the ultimate particle can be imagined as something self-sufficient and tangible. The quantum is nothing like that. It is to be recalled that in Normal Realism, the quantum is more like the transitory light-pixel, an event on a video screen. As we say, it is a unit of information of the sort from which, in statistical profusion, the physical scenario is projected.

    Also to be recalled is that in Normal Realism the quantum cannot sensibly be conceived as having any existence in isolation, as a ‘thing-in-itself’. It is never more than a terminal irreducible of the whole macro-phenomenal scenario. In that scenario the quantum is, essentially, a unit of interaction between an observer and an observed object, or between an object such as, say, the Sun and another object, a leaf, perhaps, or a solar panel or a rock. However, as has been said, in view of the integrity and instantaneity of its source-sink connection, the quantum cannot, logically, be thought of as a mediating ‘particle’, ‘wave’ or ‘wave-particle’ of any sort. As we have reasoned, the light-quantum (photum) does not ‘go’ from anywhere to anywhere. It is, so to speak, ‘there as soon as it starts out’, being, in effect, in both places at once. What we call a ‘quantum interaction’ is therefore more suitably conceived as the replacement of one photum by another in the purely sequential or ‘cinematic’ way that has been described, in which the photum as observed is the same as it occurs in the object – just like the pixel in a video scenario, which is the same event in what the viewer sees of the faraway hills as he sees on the face of the screen.

    How, then, in the same breath, can we say that the photum is both purely sequential in that ‘cinematic’ way and also a unit of physical interaction? How, in our minds, are we to accommodate, together, those two seemingly irreconcilable concept-constellations? Between what things and what other things in the real world does that quantum interaction occur in those cinematic terms? The answer Normal Realism gives is that it is between objects or agents that are ‘actors’, as it were, in the overall macro-phenomenal scenario. These macro-phenomenal interactions between real tangible things in that scenario reduce to quanta in a manner similar to what the director and viewer of a movie call ‘action’, which reduces, in the cine-camera to sequences of action-less still photographs, or stills. In each still, everything in the photograph, the objects, the actors or whatever, is instantly connected to everything else in that still, with there being no motion as such between any of those objects. All motions and interactions which take place in the interplay between the actors and other agents in the drama consist of time-sequences of these stills which, in themselves, take no part in that action. In a Western movie, for example, motions like those of the galloping horses, the arrows of the Indians, the spinning wheels of the stagecoach and so on are not part of the stills as such. These stills, in themselves, are static. They are no more than the instantaneous quantum components of the movements of the objects in the drama. Analogously, the interplay of bodies on the ordinary macro-physical level is not a characteristic of the quanta themselves. The quanta do not ‘move’, they simply occur. In other words, all ‘interaction’ properly so called, takes place on that sequential, macro-physical level, not at the level of the ‘quantum stills’ as such. The quanta, therefore, are ‘interactions’ only insofar as they are parts of macroscopic interactions, in the way that a coin may be part of a monetary transaction without being a ‘transaction’ in itself.

    In Normal Realism, then, interactions between physical objects on the macro-level, even on the astronomical scale, are reducible, at the point, or pole of observation shown in Fig. 1, Chapter 7, to interactionless quantum pixels (photo). What, then, in Normal Realism, are the ultimate objects in the macro-phenomenal ‘video-drama’ between which physical interactions take place? What, in other words, are the elemental ‘transactors’ of the quantum information? The Normal Realist answer is as follows.

    As we have seen, the universal ‘substance’ (literally a standing under) is not mass but angular momentum which is the inseparable combination of the primary physical measures, mass, length and time. This is the quantity associated with motion ordinarily observed in the orbits of moons around planets, planets around stars, stars around galaxies and so on, right throughout the whole of the natural scenario [91]. This measure is quantised in ultimately irreducible units of $\hbar = h/2\pi$. It then follows that the ‘atom’ of Normal Realism is a terminal abstraction of our experience of the ordinary macro-scale and so is perceived as an indivisible elementary angular momentum system. Thus, whereas the ultimate atom for Democritus was an irreducible, solid throughout and permanent piece of matter, or mass, for Normal Realism the ‘atom’ is no less irreducible, but is a transitory element of the true substance, angular momentum, which includes motion as an integral part of its character. In other words, the Normal realist approach turns the Democritan atom ‘inside-out’. While the consequence of interpreting the ultimate substance as ‘matter’ was the paradoxical Eleatic stasis which made motion inconceivable, the alternative of interpreting it instead as angular momentum avoids that stasis because, unlike brute matter, angular momentum incorporates motion within its very definition.

    In its simplest, most reduced form, the angular momentum quantum can be conceived as a pair of balanced ‘orbiting’ masses equivalent to the traditional, so-called ‘electron’ and ‘proton’ as in the Bohr atom, but devoid of their traditional ‘electrostatic charge’ [92]. In POAMS, an ‘atom’ is therefore a configuration of pure indivisible angular momentum in which these component masses may be conceived, simplistically, as orbiting a common centre of balance (barycentre). It needs to be stressed here however, that there is no implication in POAMS that the component masses actually orbit each other as the planets orbit the Sun on the macro-scale. The traditional ‘electrostatic charge’ on those masses, in units of coulombs, is replaced by an equivalent spin kinetic energy in ordinary mechanical units of joules. This is the spin ascribed to those particles by Uhlenbeck and Goudsmit [93]. The ‘atom’ of POAMS is therefore a combination of orbital and spin angular momentum. This it cannot be according to Newtonian ‘gravity’ theory. This inclusion of spin kinetic energy in the equation of motion determined by the total angular momentum means having to replace what is the usual value of the ‘gravitational constant’ $G$, with a variable which is equal to standard $G$ only for objects with zero spin (see Appendix 5). On the macro-phenomenal level, for the spins, of orbiting planets, satellites and so on, the difference in G, for even the fastest of these spins is miniscule. By contrast, on the micro-physical level, for ‘electrons’ and ‘protons’, spin is by far the greater portion of the overall angular momentum. As a result, compared with standard G the ‘electrodynamical’ 𝒮 is many orders of magnitude greater, which explains the immense difference between what are called the ‘electrical’ and the ‘gravitational’ forces required to remove these bodies from their natural orbits [94].

    9.2. The Normal Realist atom

    In Normal Realism, then, the ultimate ‘objects’ between which the quantum interactions take place are atoms. In summary, the difference between the POAMS atom and that of the atom conceived by Niels Bohr in conventional electrodynamics, is that in the POAMS atom there are no ‘electrically charged’ particles answering to the traditional ‘electrons’ and ‘protons’. Without that theoretical dressing, those particles become just plain masses, which are inseparable components of an integral angular momentum system, the measure of which is dependent on measures traditionally associated with mass, length and time. Nor are these atoms configurations of ‘standing waves’ as in the later theories of Schrödinger, et al. The simplest atom, as in the standard Bohr model, is that of the chemical substance hydrogen which consists of a pair of pure masses equivalent to those of the traditionally termed ‘electron’ and ‘proton’. These are in an automatically paired and balanced integral angular momentum relation of basic magnitude ℏ (½ℏ for the ‘electron’ and ½ℏ for the ‘proton’) [95]. This also includes the orbital angular momentum of those particles which is very small in magnitude in comparison with the spin angular momentum of these fundamental masses, which is relatively huge.

    It is the direct proper time-instantaneous interactions between these atoms (quantum accumulators) in action units ℏ which are the photo, the quantum pixels, statistical numbers of which constitute those informational patterns we know as light. (Actually, in Normal Realism, it would be more appropriate to say that what we call ‘light’ is ultimately reducible to these fundamental proper time-instantaneous interactions between atoms.)

    On the macro-phenomenal level, these atoms can be thought of as ‘the parts’ of distance-separated whole objects. Regardless of the distances involved, even on an intergalactic scale, the atoms transact energy directly, that is, instantly, between the object and the observer in purely disembodied integer units of nℏ, where n is a whole multiple, or integer. Like objects in a single cinematographic still, these connections between atoms are instantaneous in the sense of being timeless, or durationless. This means that the distance between, say, a galaxy and the eye of the astronomer is non-existent on that quantum-pixel level, in the same way that no distance can be projected from a single video-screen pixel or a single dot in a pointillist painting. As we say, it is only in the informational patterns of these pixels that distance is manifest. It is only in the cinematic sequences of these patterns, or quantum stills, as in a movie, that these interactions have time-delay over distance in the constant dimensional ratio, $c$, of measures in metres to measures in seconds.

    The ‘quantum still’ is therefore a pair of atoms in proper time-instantaneous contact, in which one of the atoms, $A$, say, has the higher energy while the other, $B$, has the lower energy, with neither one being the ‘cause’ of the interaction. In the next still in the cinematic sequence, some of the surfeit of energy in, say, $A$ has disappeared from $A$ and has appeared in $B$, with there being no sensible question as to how this energy has been conveyed. As is well known, there are no parameters to describe that quantum jump, either within or between the atoms, any more than there are pictures of action in the break between one cinematographic still and the next. The photum which is the jump in the energy-differential between the two states, is completely indeterminate as to when it occurs, but when it does occur it is a consummate link between an atom in the first still and another atom in the next still in the cinematic sequence.

    9.3. Real forces versus purely theoretical in vacuo forces

    We have seen that the ‘atom’ as conceived in Normal Realism is as radically different from that conceived by Bohr and his followers as Bohr’s atom is from that of Democritus.
