However, in all purely empirical or practical respects it is similar to Bohr’s, except that it is shorn of that obscuring ‘electromagnetic’ accretion. Nor does atomic interaction involve the time-delayed in vacuo ‘field-disturbances’, or ‘electromagnetic waves’ of classical conception, which are usually described in the same sort of language that is used by a facile analogy with ‘sound-waves’ etc. By contrast, in Normal Realism, every atomic interaction consists of just one single, indivisible proper time-instantaneous ‘quantum blip’, a quantum jump between one quantum still, or state, and the next in the cinematic succession in which the energy-differential between two atoms from the one still to the other is either greater or less.

    There should be nothing intrinsically puzzling about that statement as a piece of language in itself – in that particular ‘language-game’ as Wittgenstein might say. It is only when that same ‘cinematic’ piece of language is used in the same context as in ordinary experience that puzzlement arises. This is where that interaction at the quantum level translates as instantaneous action-at-a-distance, in which the ordinary meaning of ‘distance’ suggests something entirely mystifying or ‘spooky’. This is because two entirely different ‘language-games’ are thrown together without regard for their distinction. It makes the usual language of ‘Standard Model’ Quantum Physics as confusing as that of a football match described in terms of the rules of tennis or cricket.

    This is where the paradigm distinction in conception and language has to be balanced precariously in the reader’s mind between those two entirely different ‘language-games’, so that our conceptions of what goes on at the quantum level should not be described in the same words that we use on the level of our ordinary everyday experience of distance, fields, waves, particles, velocity, etc. This means dispensing, on the quantum level, with such ideas as ‘field-forces’, ‘waves in vacuo’, ‘flying photons’ and the like and thinking, instead, of atoms and the inseparable interaction-events between them, as single integrated quantum pixels from which, in statistical profusion, the world is observationally projected.

    9.3.1. The atom as a quantum accumulator

    The Normal Realist atom so far described is, perhaps, best thought of in ordinary-language terms, as a stored form of light-energy. It is an angular momentum quantum accumulator insofar as it serves as an assemblage of bits of transactable energy in hierarchical echelons of integer multiples of the action unit $h$. These increments of energy in the quantum accumulator are stored radially, like an onion, in accordance with the Normal Realist equivalent of the Balmer-Rydberg step formula (see Appendix 3). As that store of energy gets larger, so the internal ‘orbit levels’ of its mass components increase in radial amounts $nr$ where $n$ is an integer describing the particular level, and $r$ is the so-called ‘ground-level radius’ of the lowest energy state. The ‘ground-level radius’ of the Normal Realist atom is equivalent to the standard Bohr radius ($5.292 \times 10^{-11}$ metres). This atom may be simplistically conceived as being somewhat like a two-ball bolas (the ‘proton’ with a mass of $1.6726231 \times 10^{-27}$ kilograms and the ‘electron’ with a mass of $9.1093897 \times 10^{-31}$ kilograms), connected with an elastic string, where the faster the bolas rotates, the more the string stretches and the further apart the balls become. However, in the quantum accumulator there is nothing like that ‘elastic string’ holding these masses together. It is a tautology to say that in order to prevent a body from following in its natural force-free orbit, a force has to be applied. In the quantum accumulator this is no theoretical in vacuo field-force of ‘electrical attraction’ or whatever. The only force in this case is the real force which has to be exerted by any agent seeking to pull or push the mass constituents of the atom in or out of their natural orbits. This is why, when attempts are made to separate those constituents, in large accumulations of ‘electrically charged’ bodies, they resist with what has been traditionally interpreted as a ‘force of electrical attraction’, which is many orders of magnitude greater than the strength of the so-called ‘gravitational’ force. Of course, this force cannot be applied to the ‘electron’ and ‘proton’ individually, as if by pushing them together with a matchstick or pulling them apart with a tweezers. That real, measurable force is the force that is required to separate a pair of macro-physical bodies in which large numbers of these electrons and protons are segregated, respectively, into ‘negative’ and ‘positive’ charges as, for example, between the plates of a capacitor or between a cloud and the Earth during the build-up of a thunderstorm. It is to be stressed that these forces are real forces, not invisible ones acting in the void. They are measures of work actually being done by something, like the Sun in ‘blasting’ our upper atmosphere with its highly energetic ultraviolet rays to form the ionosphere which, by absorbing so much of that solar energy, protects us, here on Earth, from the most harmful of the Sun’s rays.

    It is to be emphasised that in Normal Realism, all this ‘electrical’ talk, like ‘gravitational’ talk, is redundant. This means that in Normal Realism the traditional ‘electrons’ and ‘protons’ are replaced by plain masses in the same measure as those traditionally named particles but without the esoteric concept of ‘charge’ which is replaced by angular momentum in the form of intrinsic positive or negative spin. The meaning of that word ‘charge’ is retained, however, as appropriate to macro-level collections of either positive or negative ions in the usual electrician’s sense, or the sense in which a cannon may be ‘charged’ with gunpowder. As applied to ‘electrons’ and ‘protons’ themselves, however – or grains of gunpowder – those words are inappropriate since they suggest (in the ‘electronic’ case) some unexplainable property possessed by the particles whereby they ‘pull’ or ‘push’ one another.

    The real force required to ‘ionise’ neutral atoms is traditionally called ‘electromotive’ force (e.m.f.) measured in volts. This is the force which moves these ions along wires, etc., in currents, measured in amperes (amps). Charge is the measure, in units of coulombs, of whole static collections of ions of one sign or the other, as in a capacitor. The capacity of an entity to hold or store a charge is measured in units of farads. In Normal Realism this language is virtually the same as in standard textbook physics.

    We must not, however, lose sight of the fact that, in the last analysis, all this ‘electrical’ language is about phenomena and that those measures described in the above paragraph are measures derived from the analysis of phenomena on the ordinary macro-scale. We must recall, also, that all interactions between particles are ultimately quantised in irreducible quantum jumps of action in units of Planck’s constant $h$. As already discussed, it is from our interpretations of observational patterns and sequences of these quanta that we obtain all our knowledge of nature, from microphysics to cosmology. The phenomena which have been traditionally interpreted as ‘electrical’, are manifest, for example, in rubbed amber, lightning, currents in wires, ‘static’, as in coronal discharges around high tension alternating current conductors, and so on. Certainly, it was never the case that we first saw things labelled by nature as ‘atoms’, ‘electrons’, ‘protons’ and the like, and then built up all that we know about the natural world from there.

    Having said that, it is incumbent on us to explain how these energetic interactions between atoms are connected with what we call ‘light’, those quantum pixels which are the fount of all physical knowledge. What, then, are the macro-physical phenomena from which, by rational analysis, our knowledge of these ‘electrons’ and so on stems?

    What these phenomena demonstrate is that a vibrating movement of ‘electrons’ causes the transfer of energy in quantum jumps from one place to another. In a radio transmitter, for example, a circuit consisting of coils and capacitors creates an oscillating current in that circuit, which action is conveyed by induction to an aerial-to-earth conductor and thence to a distant tuned-in receiver. This is the same as happens in the case of light, where the constituent particles of atoms (‘electrons’) oscillate between orbits, in amounts of action conveyed immediately to resonant recipient-atoms some distance away. In fact, light and radio are, basically, one and the same phenomenon, except that the frequencies of visible light are vastly greater than those of radio. Apart from that, an atom in the eye of an observer reacts to the source-atom in a micro-physical manner which is the same, in essence, as that of the macro-physical radio-transmitter and receiver. Traditionally, this, of course, has created the whole nest of problems as to how and by what means that radio transmission is mediated, by the ‘æther’, ‘electromagnetic waves’, ‘wave-particles’ or whatever, all of which is, again, redundant in Normal Realism.

    In any event, it is in this and so many other, similar, ways that, by reductive analysis of phenomena at the level of ordinary experience, such as radio transmission, electrical conduction, induction, charge, etc., we extrapolate our knowledge of the micro-world of natural phenomena. Again, it has to be emphasised that there is no way in which we can start with a knowledge of that micro-world and, from there, build up our experience of the ordinary physical world, the approach which philosophers call Reductionism. As needs to be stressed again, what we start with, inevitably and in all cases, in physical science are our interpretations of what we experience on the macro-level, by dint of observation and experimentation, mostly in the conceptual forms or templates supplied by our historical language and conventions. In Normal Realism we reinterpret these same phenomena in an altogether different, ahistorical way, more in keeping with a conceptually cleaned-up and simplified modern scientific experience than with textbook tradition.

    In short, the light by which we see things is an extremely narrow band in the full light spectrum which extends from radio and infra-red to ultraviolet and beyond. As already mentioned, it consists of those instantaneous resonant interactions (quantum jumps) between the atoms into which all physical phenomena ultimately analyse. In this particular respect, the atom of Normal Realism is the same as that of the Democritan tradition, except that in the traditional theory, because light-interaction is time-delayed on the macro-level, it is assumed to be the same on the atomic level, which is like saying that because the characters in a video drama move across the screen, the pixels move across the screen in that same way – which, needless to say, they don’t. To assume that they do would be a prime example of a category mistake, and that is the kind of mistake we make in assuming that atoms and their quantum constituents exist, interrelate and move around in the same manner as everyday physical objects.

    9.3.2. The Cinematic Model of quantum interaction

    In Normal Realism and its physics offshoot POAMS, then, on the atomic level the resonant contact between any pair of atoms is direct, proper time-instantaneous and reciprocal in accordance with Newton's third law of equal and opposite action/reaction. As we say, this contact is like the relation between a pair of actors in a still photograph or frame of a movie. In one still, for example, an actor is shown preparing to throw a punch at another. In the next and subsequent stills, the fist is shown in various positions between the assailant's body and the victim's chin. Note that in each still of this particular sequence the four objects, the two actors, the fist and the chin are all presented together, simultaneously at every stage in the action, together with the presence of any other actors and objects in the picture. In the running of the film – that is, in the sequencing of the stills, called the action – the interplay of the actors and any other objects in the scenario is, of course, not instantaneous as it is in the stills, but time-delayed, like all actions as we experience them on the ordinary macro-phenomenal level. The dimensional relation between the stills and the action is in accordance with the Pythagorean time-formula shown in Fig. 3, Chapter 7, section 7.3. For the reader's convenience, we replicate that diagram here, on the right. In this

    diagram, the vertical line is the distance, s in metres, between objects in the 'still' for t = 0 on the horizontal bottom line. The measures t along that same horizontal line then represent the time-sequencing of the stills, which is the time taken, in seconds, as recorded by the body itself in covering the observational distance s. This time t is the proper time of the motion of the body, as viewed by the observer of that motion – in his telescope, say – and $t_{R}$ , the relative time, is the temporal resultant, obtained by Pythagoras' Theorem, of the two temporal components, s/c and t (see Appendix 1).

    Note that unlike $t_{R}$ , which is specific to each and every observer, the proper time t registered by the body itself, in spectral periods, is the same, that is, invariant, for all observers of the motion regardless of relative motion between that body and the observer – or between one body and another in that same overall observer-frame.

    So far, then, the situation depicted in the Pythagorean relation shown in the ‘cinematic’ depiction is precisely in accordance with the classical Newtonian, two-dimensional graph of motion – except, of course, that all distances s in the diagram are now times $s/c$. The difference, however, between this classical, or Newtonian, graph of motion and the relativistic graph is that whereas in the Newtonian graph, there are no more than just two time-measures, in the relativistic or Pythagorean graph there are three, namely, $s/c$, $t$ and $t_{\mathrm{R}}$, all measured in seconds. Thus, whereas the two-dimensional part of the diagram is representative of the classical or Newtonian velocity $s/t$ of the body in the ‘running of the film’ – that is, the observational sequencing of the stills in the time $t$ – the third time-measure is the length $t_{\mathrm{R}}$ of the diagonal line in the diagram. The length of that diagonal line, in seconds, is the time of that body in travelling that distance $s$ as recorded by the viewer of the action, and $c$ is simply the constant converting all measures in metres into seconds. This geometrises the dimensional setup, so that all lengths become times in the distance-time relation $c$ [96].

    This, however, is where we reach the limit of our cinematic analogy. In particular, there is no way of representing, in the model, what is called the intransitivity of quantum interaction (see Chapter 8, section 8.2.1). Quantum interactions (quantum stills) are discrete and intransitive, which simply means that when atom $A$ is interacting with atom $B$, and atom $B$ is interacting with atom $C$, it doesn’t necessarily mean that atom $A$ is interacting with atom $C$ – not least because the intermediate atom $B$ cannot both emit and absorb a quantum of energy in one and the same instance. So, while on the quantum level every interacting pair of atoms is in the same now as each other, this same now cannot be extended to any, far less all, other atoms in that same scenario. This is why, in Normal Realism, there is no continuity problem, or ‘Eleatic stasis’ to be considered.

    This intransitivity of quantum interaction cannot be squared with the cinematic analogy. This is because the quantum stills do not follow one another in a regular sequence, one after another, like the photographic frames in a reel of film. Rather, the sequences of quantum stills are scrambled, or randomised, so that the progression of the ‘film’ consists of time-sequences of statistical (i.e., information-statistical) numbers of these quantum stills in accordance with the statistical Second Law of Thermodynamics, as discussed in Chapter 5, section 5.3.1. The distance-time progression rate, $c$, of the quantum stills is customarily called the ‘speed of light’. This is the asymptotic ‘speed limit’ of all interactions in the cinematic scenario. Thus, whereas the individual quantum interactions are instantaneous, their statistical successions are causally limited to the finite distance-time rate $c$, so that, once again, there is no ‘EPR’ conflict between instantaneous and time-delayed interaction to bemuse us [40].

    In Normal Realism, then, there is no need whatsoever to think of everything having to be connected in the single, overall now that classical physicists and astronomers talk about. That is to say, in Normal Realism there is no ‘behind the scenes’ present moment in which, as it were, the whole universe ages at the same rate in some imagined ‘absolute’ time which ‘marches on’ regardless of whatever differences there might be in the various clock-readings and ageings (see Chapter 8, section 8.3). The theory of Relativity has revealed that this is definitely not the case. Things simply do not age at the same rate everywhere in any metaphysical ‘God’s time’, in the way the anti-Relativity ‘Dissidents’ (followers of Herbert Dingle) imagine. So there is no meaning in the phrase ‘the age of the universe’. Consequently, in looking at distant objects it makes no sense to say – as so many people do – that we are ‘looking back in time’, either a second, a minute or fourteen billion years – as some astronomers estimate from the moment of the alleged ‘Big Bang’. As soon as it happens, every millisecond that ticks-by right now becomes history which is straightaway inaccessible to us, either visually or instrumentally. In other words, whatever is done is done and cannot be undone, and there is no possible way of ‘looking at it’ as though looking directly at the past, an assumption which automatically accompanies the traditional notion of light as having a ‘speed’ in space.

    Questions might arise, as they have in Philosophy for generations, as to the time-length, or temporal thickness of what has been called the ‘specious present’, so named by an amateur philosopher, E. R. Kelly, and developed by the American philosopher, William James, in 1893. In Normal Realism, the present moment is simply the moment in which, at the quantum level, some atom in an object and an atom in our eye or some optical instrument, interact. The duration, or temporal ‘thickness’, of that present moment is the time of one quantum cycle, which is the inverse of its spectral frequency. What has been called the ‘specious present’ is then the statistical number of such quantum interactions (quantum stills) which are required to trigger, in our nerve endings and the subsequent knock-on neural processes, in the brain, what we normally call ‘seeing’. It is these brain-patterns and sequences of interactions with other objects, other observers and other instruments, that constitute what our language labels as phenomena which, as already discussed, are our primary contact with objective reality from which all else is extrapolated.

    To all intents and purposes, then, apart from these conceptual differences, the divergence between the Normal Realist and the standard orthodox account of atoms and atomic interactions might be regarded as almost trivial, especially regarding the standard experimental results. This, of course, is the case only superficially. So far we have said nothing about nucleonics, concerning those exotic so-called ‘elementary particles’, such as ‘gluons’, ‘quarks’, ‘bosons’ ‘fermions’ and so on, that are said to have been ‘discovered’ at our reductionist ‘atom-smashing’ laboratories. However, in this book it is not our business to reproduce lists of these mostly theoretical entities in that specialised area of research. Our only agreement with it which is relevant at this particular stage is, as V. F. Weisskopf, the Founding Director of the atomic research laboratory CERN, once admitted to Pope: ‘Certainly none of the experiments, either in this or in any other laboratory, has succeeded in “splitting h”!’ So, despite their radical differences in so many other respects, Normal Realism and modern particle physics are in agreement that, at least, the modern equivalent of the unsplittable material atom of Democritus is the no less unsplittable quantum of action h.

    Returning, now, to the question of causality raised in Chapter 5, section 5.3.1., by far the most significant difference between the classical and the Normal Realist view lies in their respective interpretations of physical causality. In classical physics, it is generally assumed that the causes of everything lie, ultimately, in ‘hidden mechanisms’ of Democritan conception where it is assumed that, somehow, the random clattering together of these alleged solid and self-sufficient particles causes us to exist, along with all that we see and sense around us. This, in some entirely unexplained way, is supposed to include all our ideas of beauty and justice, our loves and hates, all our social and moral virtues, values and institutions and, indeed, even life itself. It is, basically, the common assumption on which the so-called ‘atom smashing’ laboratories operate. As the director Weisskopf described it during the ceremony marking the 25th anniversary of the research centre, CERN, the object of that kind of research is:

    ‘… to study the ultimate constituents of matter and the ultimate forces of nature, the driving forces of all natural processes’$^{[97]}$.

    The way in which Normal Realism differs from this reductionist, bottom-up view of causality is altogether profound, as may be seen in Chapter 12. Meanwhile, let us deal with an issue that is of some serious concern.

    9.4. The psycho-social hurdle to paradigm change

    Undoubtedly, the biggest problem posed by this suggested ‘new physics’ paradigm is what someone has dubbed ‘the Intellectual Problem’. This is the problem of having to grapple with sets of ideas for which standard words in traditional-physics are used in entirely new and unfamiliar ways.

    Think of the difference between the language which is used by the actors in a movie drama and the language used by the camera crew and the director of the film. Instructions such as ‘action!’ ‘cut!’ ‘pan left’, etc., between the film director and the camera team obviously have no place in the drama as such. If mixed together into the same production the result would be ridiculous. It is plain, therefore that some uses of language may be complementary but should never be conflated. Such are the language games we use to describe events at the quantum-analytical level and those we use to describe objects, events and processes on the ordinary physical level. Regardless of how perfectly suitable each of these uses may be in itself, when thrown indiscriminately together they create nothing but confusion.

    This is the situation with which our traditional physics presents us. In our accustomed physics language we talk about ‘fields’, ‘waves’, ‘photons’, and so on, all in the same breath (i.e., the same language category) as we talk about corn-fields, sea-waves, bullets, billiard-balls, etc. Quantum physicists talk about theoretical things called ‘quarks’ which they describe as having such properties as ‘colour’, ‘charm’, ‘nakedness’, ‘upness’, ‘downness’, ‘strangeness’ and so on, in a travesty of ordinary language which makes a complete mockery of commonsense. This entirely dysfunctional lack of distinction in traditional physics between the language it employs in speaking about ordinary physical phenomena and the language it has produced to describe things on the analytical micro-level, creates what logicians call ‘category mistakes’. These are analogous to failing to distinguish between the meanings of, say, ‘put the light out’ and ‘put the cat out’ or between an amount of courage and an amount of beans in a jar. An example of this sort of confusion, in the context of modern physics is to list interactions between particles as ‘particles’ in the same language-category as meteorites, cannon balls and planets. A prime example of this sort of category conflation is provided by the descriptions of the so-called ‘photons,’ ‘mesons’, ‘gluons’, etc. as ‘particles’. For instance, we have seen that there can be no such things as ‘photons’, the alleged mediating particles between atoms. ‘Mesons’ are described in the same way as mediators between the sub-atomic mass-constituents of atoms (nucleons). ‘Gluons’ are the supposed mediators of the hypothetical ‘force’ that ‘glues’ together the theoretical particles called ‘quarks’. These quasi-particles exist by virtue of simply taking some energy involved in the various ‘exchange forces’, dividing it by $c^2$ and calling it a ‘particle’, which is like reporting a collision between two road vehicles as another – that is, a third – road-vehicle, hence as a collision between three vehicles. Imagine the confusion this would create in a magistrate’s court! Little wonder that, as stated in Chapter 4, no less an authority than the famous physicist, Richard Feynman, stated: ‘I think it’s safe to say that no one understands quantum mechanics’ [98]. But how else can it be when the language in which its ideas are expressed is just sheer gobbledygook?

    Most pertinent, then, to the theme of this book is the growing tendency of some of our most highly qualified physics celebrities to fail to distinguish, in language, between a world of theory and a world of fact. No intelligent commonsense users of language should ever fool themselves in this way. Contemporary modern physicists, however, with their cavalier attitude towards commonsense, let their purely artificial language in expressing such concepts as ‘The Big Bang’, ‘dark matter’, ‘photons’, ‘gluons’, ‘wave-particles’, ‘WIMPS’ (Weakly Interacting Mass Particles), the ‘God Particle’ and such like, run riot, allowing different language-categories to spread into one another like ink on blotting paper. Encouraged by the thirst of our modern media for sheer novelty and the lionising of science celebrities, this both creates and sustains the confusion that is our extant Modern Physics. It is this sort of confusion that Normal Realism seeks to eradicate by its logical/linguistic sorting-out process described in Chapter 5.

    Such, is the ‘intellectual problem’, which is the stress that Normal Realism places on the reader to discriminate between the language-uses in describing things real and things purely theoretical or speculative. It is to realise that in the end-analysis, things like ‘electrons’, ‘protons’, ‘force-fields’ and so on are not real objects, labelled as such by nature, but theoretical ones. The real objects are those things and processes which are observed, either directly or indirectly by means of our telescopes, microscopes and other instruments, and interpreted in the most logically consistent and conceptually economical manner.

    In summary, then, 20$^{th}$ and 21$^{st}$ century science’s most spectacular failure to exercise control over this wanton proliferation of jargon has allowed us to imagine all sorts of things and situations which should be categorised as Fiction. Such a lack of category distinction by the reader seeking to understand this Normal Realist thesis would be disastrous for the outcome of this present exercise. In order to avoid that befuddlement, in Normal Realism the language that applies to what has been described as the underlying ‘cinematic’ process and the language that is used in relation to the physical scenario have to be kept separate. The onus, therefore, on any reader wishing to understand Normal Realism, is to bear in mind, throughout the discussion, the distinction between the two language-categories employed in talking about physical phenomena such as trees, ducks and barns on the one hand and, on the other hand, their projected constituents such as ‘atoms’, ‘electrons’, ‘quarks’ and so on. These things cannot sensibly be thought of as existing all in one and the same material world as, unfortunately, traditional physics has taught us to think since the time of Democritus.

    The ‘big trick’ in understanding the ‘cinematic’ analogy is to emphasise this category-distinction in language between talking about physical interaction in quantum-still terms and action terms, is to be able to switch with facility between, on the one hand, thinking of the world in our usual way as an actor involved in the play and, on the other hand, thinking of it in the role of the film-maker or the cinema projectionist. This former way of thinking and speaking is as a Relativist and the latter as a Quantum Theorist. With this facility firmly in mind, the ‘intellectual problem’ is eased and so, automatically, are so many of the other problems that bedevil our modern understanding of physical phenomena.

    CHAPTER 10

    The Logical ‘Flipside’ to the Old Physics Paradigm

    10.1. The holistic approach to micro-physics

    To recapitulate what has been discussed so far, in Normal Realism the phenomenon of light-interaction reduces to statistical – sometimes informational-statistical – numbers of what, by analogy with cinematography (the ‘Cinematic Model’), we have called ‘quantum stills’. In one still is an atom which is at one quantum-level of energy and in the next still that same atom is at a different quantum level, with nothing happening in between. From the one still to the next still, the state of the atom has changed, immediately, so that in the quantum jump between those stills, a quantum of energy is either lost or gained by that atom. The conservation principle demands that this released or captured energy-difference (action) in a star, say, is immediately and directly compensated by a complementary and fully consummated quantum jump in another atom somewhere else – for example, in an eye, a camera or a spectrometer. This synchronous proper time-instantaneous at-a-distance relation between bodies is what constitutes the ‘quantum pixel’ that appears in the light-spectrum in accordance with the onion-like Balmer-Rydberg distribution (see Appendix 3).

    Let us remind the reader what these ‘atoms’ referred to in the Cinematic Model are. In Chapter 9, section 9.3.1, the Normal Realist atom was described as a quantum accumulator, which is how it may be described in the simplest and most conceptually parsimonious interpretation of the phenomena. This is not as a solid-all-through piece of matter, like the atom of Democritus. It is more like an emptiness, or ‘hole’, an ultimate limit of reducibility in our analysis of matter. This is explained as follows.

    From the standpoint of the ahistorical, Normal Realist alternative to conventional micro-physics, it is clear that what Democritus might have done in order to solve the Eleatic stasis, or immobility problem, was simply to adopt the inverse of his historic solution. Thus, whereas that solution was to put material substance on the inside of the atom and the void on the outside, he might, alternatively, and just as logically, have put the void on the inside of the atom and the material substance on the outside. This would have solved – or, rather, dissolved – the philosophically enervating ‘Problem of Knowledge’ discussed earlier (in Chapter 2, section 2.2).

    There would then have been no alleged nether-world of the sort we can never directly observe but only theorise about. There would have been simply the one and only ordinary world of physical phenomena that stands right there before us every waking moment, the world of space, time and matter, of things with all their physical dimensions, dispositions, properties and qualities. So far as Normal Realism is concerned, this is the truly real world we directly and unproblematically perceive, the ordinary world that J. L. Austin described (see Chapter 5, section 5.1). In this immanently real world, space, as we know it, is not an external self-sufficient, self-enduring void, separate from matter. Together with matter it is an integral part of the whole phenomenal scenario as manifest as the phenomenon of space, matter and motion which is all angular momentum of one sort or another. Aristotle thought much the same. ‘If space is truly nothing’, he argued, ‘then to say that things are separated by space is to say, literally, that they are separated by nothing, which is the same as saying that they are not separated’.

    As befits what we have said, then, about the proper use of language, space cannot be nothing, as Aristotle averred. Any dimensions these voids or spaces have, they do not possess in themselves but only in the context of phenomena as a whole, including physical bodies and space as integral parts of these same physical phenomena. So, other than with reference to physical bodies there is no self-existing, self-extended and self-enduring space or void that it makes sense to speak of. This is also the basis of what is known as ‘Mach’s Principle’, which holds, in effect, that everything in the universe is, in some sense or other, connected to and dependent on everything else.

    In our Normal Realist alternative to conventional physics, then, everything is interconnected – in proper time, that is (see Appendix 1). This overall connectedness reduces, on analysis, to intransitive and therefore statistical or probabilistic connections between atoms. These are the same as the atoms of standard atomic physics except for the vital difference that our alternative ‘atom’ is not a measure of plain solid mass. It is an angular momentum quantum accumulator constituted of the inseparable combination of measures of mass, length and time whose ultimately irreducible unit is $h$, the fundamental unit of angular momentum. Between one atom and some other atom elsewhere, energy is transacted in instantaneous amounts of inter-resonant action, $nh$ (note: action in units of $nh$, not angular momentum in units of $h$). This Normal Realist ‘atom’, therefore, cannot be sensibly depicted as something which can be picked up and inspected as an isolated and independent bit of mass analogous to a pebble or a billiard ball. It is what might be conceived, rather, as the static or ‘frozen’, form of light-energy, layered and coloured – yes, coloured, in accordance with proper ordinary language usage of that term and with the Balmer-Rydberg series of lines in the colour spectrum (see Appendix 3).

    In terms, then, of what properties, qualities or whatever should this alternative ‘atom’ be described? The answer is, in itself, none. All definitions, descriptions and other predications regarding it have to be in terms, not of this object as a ‘thing-in itself’ but of the world around it, that is, the world of physical phenomena in which it inheres. Even in experiments claiming to deal with individual atoms, the manifestations of these entities are describable only within the holistic context of the experimental apparatuses and the general environs in which the event takes place $^{[46]}$. In this whole-world nexus the atom analyses-out into the terminally irreducible quanta of angular momentum $\hbar$.

    It needs to be emphasised, here, that the expressions $nh$ and $\hbar$ do not signify different quanta. They both denote the same quantity of energy but in different forms. The one, $nh$, is the disembodied (i.e., massless) interactive form of light energy, or action, that is transacted between these atoms, and the other, $\hbar$, is that same amount of energy stored, or ‘frozen’ in the embodied, or ‘captured’, mass of the angular momentum quantum accumulator. Nor can this quantum accumulator be conceived in the classical way as some thing-in-itself, with its mass components whirling away metaphysically ‘behind the scenes’, waiting for its cue to pop out ‘on stage’ and release its energy. It exists only as and when it takes part in some action or other, which is at some indeterminate place and time located only probabilistically in the overall scenario.

    In these terms, then, the ‘atom’ of POAMS cannot be thought of as comparable with that conceived by Democritus. Angular momentum is every bit as directly observational (hence substantial in the phenomenalist sense) as ‘matter’ was ever supposed to be. There is, then, nothing abstract or ‘metaphysical’ about angular momentum. It is something that astronomers and factory engineers understand and deal with on a regular observational basis. This, of course, is on the ordinary practical level of physical phenomena in comparison with which the underlying quantum discontinuities are so small and fine-grained as not normally to be observed.

    On that ordinary, macro-phenomenal level, then, the primary reality as far as Normal Realism is concerned, is angular momentum which, by definition, is a paired and reciprocally balanced relation between masses where the motion of the one mass is instantly and automatically correlated with that of a partner around a common centre of balance called the fulcrum or barycentre. This can be observed in the planets and satellites of the solar system, the motion of every one of which is correlated with that of each and every other in that paired and balanced way. This is evident in the phenomenon known to astronomers as perturbation, in which the presence of bodies which may be invisible to us can be detected by their observed effect on those which are visible. As is well known, this is the way in which the planet Neptune, normally invisible to the naked eye, was discovered, in 1846, from observations of its perturbation by neighbouring and nearer-to-Earth planet, Uranus.

    It is by extrapolating downwards from motion on this macro-phenomenal level towards the ultimate micro-phenomenal level that we approach the limit of reduction at which the mass components of angular momentum are not visible or tangible, or even describable, in the ordinary way. In the history of physics, attempts at reduction have uncovered these sub-atomic mass components which, as we have seen, have been given names, such as ‘electron’, ‘proton’, ‘neutron’, etc. These are classically conceived as orbiting one another in a balanced angular momentum system in which the electrons orbit the proton by analogy with the way in which the planets orbit the Sun. The difference is that at that fundamental level, not only do these ‘orbits’ exist only as part of the indivisible angular momentum system, but also that the ‘radii’ of the orbits increase and decrease in discrete series, or ‘quantum jumps’ with the absorption or release, respectively, of transacted, disembodied light energy in action units $h$. This is without any particle or anything else in any way traversing the gaps between. It is these implicitly paired and balanced systems of ‘mass-particles’ which are the purely formal components of angular momentum in the ‘atom’ of Normal Realism and its physics spin-off, POAMS.

    It is to be emphasised that in POAMS, unlike the Democritan atoms these alternative ‘atoms’ do not exist autonomously, or independently of one another, far less as the ‘building-blocks’ of physical reality. They are all integrated and inseparable parts of the universal angular momentum nexus of which these atoms are the terminal irreducibles of all physical phenomena.

    Recall, then, that in Normal Realism, the customary conception of these implicit ‘particles’ as either ‘electrically charged’ or ‘electrically neutral’, is redundant, since it can be shown that angular momentum is sufficient in itself to provide all the parameters of their ‘orbits’ without that sort of theoretical elaboration (see Appendix 4). Accordingly, we jettison the customary ‘electrodynamics’ language and regard all these fundamental entities, whether they have been named ‘electrons’, ‘protons’, ‘neutrons’, or whatever, as being, in themselves, no more than just different measures in the category of kilograms within systems of angular momentum on the lowest levels of reductive analysis.

    10.2. What, really, are ‘Black Holes’?

    We repeat that in Normal Realism, ‘atoms’ as such cannot sensibly be conceived in the Democritan way as solid-throughout and determinately located bits of matter, nor in the ‘atom-smashing’ way to be cracked open like nuts to reveal their kernels. By far the larger portion of those ‘insides’ is not matter but void. Take, for instance, the case of an Olympic hammer-thrower. When he swings his hammer it is plain that neither he nor the hammer occupy the centre of the motion. They both wobble around a common centre of balance, or barycentre, which in itself is empty of matter. Typically, then, in systems of more or less equal-sized bodies there is a ‘hole’ at the centre. Examples of this are the vortex of a whirlpool, the ‘eye’ of a cyclone or the ‘Black Hole’ at the centre of a spiral galaxy.

    Note that unlike Plate 2, the hole, in the galaxy in Plate 3, is invisible due to being overwhelmed by the amount of light from the centre of the galaxy. Nevertheless the hole is generally deemed to be there, for standard physical reasons.

    These holes or hollows are an integral part of all angular momentum systems, from atoms to galaxies. No-one possessed of an ounce of commonsense would ever imagine that the centre of balance or barycentre of an angular momentum system of freely orbiting bodies was some kind of detached object existing in itself, with a mass and ‘gravitational attraction’ of its own, responsible for the orbital motions of those bodies.

    Typical also of such a system is that in losing angular momentum, by whatever means, that system shrinks towards the centre. A whirlpool, for instance, as it loses angular momentum – and energy – spirals towards its centre, faster and faster, as the liquid drains down a sinkhole of some kind. A tornado loses angular momentum through friction as its air molecules are sucked upwards through the funnel it forms in the atmosphere, and a spiral galaxy loses angular momentum as it sheds light and other radiation on a prodigiously large scale from all over. In none of these instances need we ever think of the centre of motion as some kind of autonomous object, capable of travelling around on its own in space. (This strange idea is expanded upon in Chapter 11.)

    Nothing is more puzzling, therefore, than to be told that such barycentres are ‘holes in space’, as autonomous objects – or ‘singularities’ in the jargon of the cosmologists. These are the kinds of distortions of language (category confusions) that we find in Modern Physics, which produce nothing but mystification and general gobbledygook. For instance, since space is a vacuum (says the commonsense thinker) how can we talk sensibly of a ‘hole’ in a vacuum? In works of fiction and ‘Gee Whiz’ psuedo-science, such conundrums may be useful for purposes of entertainment, but hardly in any sane and serious context of empirical science.

    10.3. Neutron stars and ‘gravitational collapse’

    In the domain of research into astronomical phenomena it is sometimes difficult to distinguish fact from fiction and true photographic images from speculative artists’ impressions and computer-graphic representations. For this reason there are no firm, absolutely categorical statements available as to what really is what in this astronomical realm. However, it seems fairly certain that, as our best telescopes reveal, there are astronomical objects with a calculated mass very much larger than our Sun’s, but which are no larger, perhaps, than a fair-sized city. If this is truly what is observed, then it fits beautifully with the general predictions of the Normal Realist angular momentum synthesis.

    In Normal Realism, what separates bodies and keeps them apart is, as we have seen, angular momentum. Like any other source of potential energy, as it loses that energy into the environment, an angular momentum system is subject to the Law of Entropy, which means that its energy gradually runs down and is eventually exhausted. It follows, then, that as angular momentum between bodies is lost, the distance between those bodies automatically diminishes, and as that distance diminishes, not only do those systems become more compacted but also the orbital speeds and kinetic energies of those compacted bodies typically increase, hence the greater, relativistically speaking, becomes their mass.

    The centre of orbital rotation (the barycentre) of such balanced systems is therefore the theoretical disappearing point of diminishing angular momentum. It also imposes the theoretical limit, $c$, of the speed of an orbiting body, as well as imposing the upper limit of the increase in its relativistic mass. In its final stages of collapse, therefore, with the distance between the orbiting stars etc., approaching zero, those stellar components of the angular momentum system must coincide, that is to say, merge, so far as is possible, into a single mass object. With further loss of angular momentum between them, the ‘electrons’ and ‘protons’ in that combined mass must finally collapse together to form ‘neutrons’ with the result that they coalesce into a ‘neutron star’ with its typically large and highly condensed mass.

    Typical also of an angular momentum system, is that as the potential energy of the system decreases, with the result that the velocity and therefore the kinetic energy of the orbits increase, this is accompanied by step-changes in the composition of a star as its component ‘nucleons’ fall closer together on the way down towards the star’s demise. In Normal Realism there are no ‘in vacuo forces’, such as ‘gravitation’, responsible for this collapse; the loss of angular momentum in itself provides a full and sufficient explanation of that process. At a critical level, the increase in kinetic energy of the nuclear components cannot be sustained, which means that the star explodes into its environment in the form of a supernova, leaving, perhaps, a remnant of the star as a relatively tiny but incredibly dense, neutronic core. Another manifestation of this postulated collapse is the appearance of accompanying X-rays and radio frequency emissions of bodies as they accelerate more and more violently into the well of annihilation.

    What, then, can these voraciously matter-consuming objects be? We contend that they are not autonomous ‘Black Holes’ as popularly proposed, which is literal nonsense. What else, then, can they be but these neutron stars just now described?

    There simply is no other logical candidate that it makes sense to speak of. However, a neutron star is not initially completely ‘black’. After the initial collapse of the star it continues to emit light, albeit on a much lower level of brightness than hitherto. This diminishes further and further as the star gradually cools until it becomes completely dark, a dead star, in effect, with huge mass and no visible quality, its presence manifest only by the rapidly swirling orbital motions of visible objects around it, such as stars, gas clouds, etc., as they are (supposedly) sucked in towards it.

    This ‘dead star’ has enormous mass, small size, tremendous density and virtually zero internal angular momentum. Regardless, however, of its lack of internal angular momentum, this neutron star remains a mass object in itself within the external angular momentum nexus as a whole. The orbital angular momentum equations (Appendix 4) show that in the neighbourhood of such a super-massive but inert body, the orbiting speeds of the surrounding bits of matter – stars, planets, space-dust and whatever – have to be huge. To the creative imagination this suggests the appearance of a galactic whirlpool sucking in stars, gases and other bits of space debris, like some deadly cosmic ‘maelstrom’ dragging those stars and whatnot to their doom into depths unknown [99]. Nor is there any mysterious ‘limbo of the lost’ into which stars etc. disappear, or ‘wormholes’ to account for the disappearance of those bits of matter into different parts of the universe as popularly conceived. They go nowhere unless it be into the neutron star, to swell, by accretion, its size and mass until it bursts out once again to become the stuff of other stars and so on in the cosmic recycling process.

    Now a ‘Black Hole’, so called, is supposed to be an unknown and unknowable source of ‘gravitational attraction’ so strong as to form a kind of wall-of-death horizon out of which ‘not even light can climb’. The associated speculative theory, of course, makes no sense whatsoever in Normal Realism, not least insofar as it employs the notion of light travelling. Not to be confused with these neutron stars are the supermassive ‘Black Holes’ which form the barycentres of galaxies in the way that has been described in the previous section of this present chapter. The masses ascribed to these galactic ‘Black Holes’ – as distinct from the neutronic ‘Black Holes’ – are very much larger because they are comparable to the size and mass of a whole galaxy, whereas a stellar ‘Black Hole’ is comparable with no more than the size and mass of a single star.

    10.4. ‘Black holes’ at the centres of atoms

    From angular momentum between stars we now switch to the opposite extreme of considering the centre of motion and balance, or barycentre, between those two masses in the atom, conventionally named the ‘electron’ and ‘proton’.

    Since the proportion of mass to volume within the atom is altogether miniscule, the amount of sheer emptiness within the atom is relatively huge. It is standard knowledge that as the atom – of hydrogen, say – absorbs more and more quanta of energy, so its mass components become more and more widely separated, whence that void gets larger and larger in quantum jumps. Eventually it gets so large as to separate the component masses altogether in what is known as ‘ionisation’. Those separated masses, customarily called ‘ions’, are traditionally conceived as being captured by other ions of opposite sign to form other atoms in what is known as chemical valency – analogous to changing partners in Country-and-Western dancing.

    These ions are therefore never absolutely separate. In the same way that there cannot be an up without a down, or a left without a right, the presence of an ion of one sign signifies that another, of opposite sign, must exist somewhere. In traditional terms, this is to say that wherever there is a positive charge (collection of positive ions) there must also be, somewhere, an equal and opposite, negative charge, where the two, by the Law of Entropy, constantly seek togetherness, or ‘neutrality’ – which is why these charges of opposite sign are said to ‘attract’. It is also the reason why charges of the same sign add, increasing the angular momentum whence they are said to ‘repel’. This is the reason why, according to POAMS, in ‘electrical’ terms, ‘opposite charges attract and like charges repel’. (We repeat that in this present context the word ‘charge’ suitably describes a collection of ions, as in ‘charging’ a cannon with gunpowder, or ‘charging’ an amount of money, etc. In this sense, ‘charge’ is an ordinary synonym of words like ‘load’ and ‘suffuse’ which is in no way esoteric in the way that classical physics implies.) The same applies, on a smaller scale, to magnets in which the positive and negative spins of ‘electrons’ form regions of charge which attract or repel one another, respectively. This electron-to-electron attraction and repulsion in ‘magnetism’, is predictably weaker than that of the ion-to-ion attraction and repulsion in ‘electrical’ charges, because the strength to be overcome, of the attachment of ‘electrons’ to ‘protons’, is far greater than that of opposite-spin electrons-to-electrons.

    As for ‘protons’ and ‘electrons’, then, no matter how energetically these masses are driven apart (ionised), they never get to roam around self-sufficiently and independently in space. They remain always, incipiently, a part of one orbital angular momentum system or another within the nexus as a whole. In Normal Realist terms, ionisation is a process of interaction between atoms. Energetic ions separated from their opposite numbers in the Sun are captured by the Earth’s angular momentum in what is customarily called its ‘magnetic field’ to create those phenomena we know as the Aurora Borealis and Aurora Australis, imaginatively described as due to a ‘solar wind’. Other phenomena, such as the plasma trails of comets, which always point away from the Sun, certainly give the superficial impression of being blown that way by a ‘wind’ of some kind [100]. Other highly energetic events in space, for example, in stars and supernovae at galactic and extragalactic distances, may eject all sorts of ionised particles to be detected in one way or another here on Earth. All these phenomena strongly suggest that the so-called ‘fundamental particles’, once ejected from their source, may travel on independently in the way we think of bullets fired from guns. It is to be recalled, however, that on that fundamental level, all such actions are, basically, quantum interactions, and that there is no such thing as distance-separation on that ultimate quantum level; that on that level all quantum actions over any distance whatsoever are proper time-instantaneous.

    So even over these greatest of distances on the macro-phenomenal scale, all elementary particle interactions whatsoever have to be complete and consummate in what is mystifyingly called instantaneous action-at-a-distance. In the jargon of quantum physics this is usually discussed in esoteric terms such as ‘quantum entanglement’, ‘non-locality’, etc., etc. All that this arcane language comes down to in the end is the simple, experimentally proven fact, that when a particle with zero spin, say, is split in two, the spin direction of the one fragment instantly determines the opposite spin-direction of the other fragment, regardless of their distance apart, since conservation demands that the opposite spins of those fragments must add-up to the zero spin of the original particle. This is said to confound Einstein’s relativistic rule that no information may be conveyed over a distance ‘faster than the speed of light’, which precipitated a running controversy between Einstein and Bohr over what is called the ‘EPR’ (Einstein, Podolski and Rosen) paradox of quantum instantaneity. (Einstein disparagingly called this alleged quantum instantaneous connection ‘spooky action-at-a-distance’.)

    According to the standard laws of conservation, no ‘elementary particle’ can leave a source without an assured billet for it in some place or other [101]. Then for a ‘cosmic-ray particle’, say, to leave a source and travel on indefinitely in an outgoing, completely random, adventurous way, without any immediately assured billet for it somewhere, would mean, nonsensically, that many of these particles would never be received but would carry on forever in some metaphysical limbo beyond reach of the laws of physics. Logically, then, the tracks that these particles make in bubble-chambers, cloud-chambers and so on cannot be caused by any conceivably aimless, isolated and independently wandering particles. The Indeterminacy Principle (sometimes called the Uncertainty Principle) rules out any sensible view of these particles travelling autonomously, all alone in vast reaches of space in some well-defined way, ‘known only to God’. (This is the way in which the Mechanists, such as Boltzmann and Laplace thought about these atomic interactions.)

    What is it, then, that forms those clear-cut tracks in bubble-chambers, etc.? Physics tells us that they are those ions – mostly ‘protons’ but sometimes ‘electrons’ and alpha particles (helium nuclei) – which are released like shrapnel from highly energised atoms. We must recall, however that in the void of space there are no such ‘tracks’. Moreover, any tracks that may be extrapolated are indeterminate, which means that they cannot be sensibly conceived as having clear-cut, well defined paths such as those of bullets and other macro-physical projectiles. This makes it true to say that in vacuo those particles have no definable tracks at all. The cosmic-ray particles and their alleged ‘tracks’ become real only as and when they are manifest in some empirical way, for instance, when they interact with something, say the atoms of air, an eye or some other detecting medium like the liquid hydrogen in a bubble-chamber, or the trail of a comet. (Sophisticated readers may, perhaps, see some vague similarity, here, between this reification of particles at the point of observation and the Copenhagen approach to Quantum Mechanics with its theory of the ‘collapse of the wave-function’.)

    In any case, not even for those most highly energetic ‘cosmic-ray particles’, or the particles accelerated by magnets in proton synchrotrons, can there be any one-ended unconsummated interactions, which would be as impossible to contemplate as a one-ended ball of string or coil of wire. These would have to be miracles of non-obedience to the various laws of conservation. The first bubble in the trail of bubbles in a bubble-chamber, therefore, has to be the product of some fully consummated, proper time-instantaneous quantum interaction between one atom, somewhere far off, and another atom at the commencement of the cascade of bubbles within the hydrogen liquid, with its secondary effect on neighbouring atoms throughout the trail in accordance with the characteristics of those interactions within the angular momentum environment. The same applies in the case of the plasma plumes of comets, which point radially away from the Sun. That is to say, every atom in those trails is in completely consummated proper time-instantaneous quantum contact with some other atom in the Sun. As physical phenomena, then, for all their apparent autonomy, like ‘Black Holes’, cosmic-ray particles and so on, those comet trails are undoubtedly due, not to being ‘knocked’ off the comet by any ‘solar wind’. Like all other physical phenomena, these appearances are observational projections from quantum pixels in the ‘cinematic’ way that has been described in Chapter 9, subsection 9.3.2. Part of this phenomenological scenario are, of course, truly space-travelling particles, of cosmic dust, micro-asteroids, etc., which shower our planet continually and may be encountered by spacecraft sometimes having a damaging effect on those vehicles. Every one of these truly space-travelling dust-grains has a trajectory determined by the whole angular momentum nexus, like those of much larger bodies. Also, like the planet Neptune, they may exist floating in space for millennia, unobserved, or perhaps never to be observed, in which case they are nevertheless part and parcel of the overall phenomenal scenario, with their presence signified, like so many other objects in that scenario, by their observed physical effects rather than directly.

    10.4.1. No need for mystery

    How can this viewpoint be in any way confusing? The reason is that it is all an issue of uses and misuses of language. In view of what was discussed in Chapter 9, section 9.3., in describing the recommended paradigm shift, it falls to us to distinguish, amongst the mixture of traditional language usages, the one which is most appropriate at any particular moment. For instance, can it be appropriate to speak of ‘atoms’, as material objects in the traditional textbook way? Or is it more appropriate to speak of them as ‘quantum accumulators’ in the new, Normal Realist way? Given the situation as it stands there is, so to speak, no ‘whistle’ that can be blown to signify, at any juncture, the language-category we should be using in that particular case. In any transitional state of language there is bound to be this sort of confusion, which puts the onus on the reader to make the appropriate switch wherever required. Inevitably, this is the situation we face whenever new experiences demand some radical or revolutionary change in the concept system, or language structure – such as, for example, during the long and uneasy transition from Medieval, Earth-centred descriptions of God’s creation to that of a universe in which our Earth and all its religions is no more than one mote of dust among countless trillions of others like it.

    Bearing this in mind, it is significant to note, in passing, that the quantum interactions between the various atoms in the trail of bubbles in a bubble-chamber leave no trails or any other sign of their existence in those gaps. Note, also, that these so-called ‘photons’ leave no tracks in vacuo, all of which supports what we have said about the discreteness and purely cinematic character of quantum events. This means that the transfer of the energy from each atom to the next in the succession of bubbles in the chamber takes place instantly in the language applied to the proper time (that is, the ‘still-frame’, or quantum time) of the action itself, while in the language of the observer time (the cinematic sequence-time) it takes place at the relative ‘speed $c$’. In some cases, these interactions, quaintly called ‘collisions’, separate (that is ‘ionise’) the particles of the liquid. These then interact in angular momentum terms with the lined-up electrons in the hugely powerful magnets surrounding the chamber to form those curves and whorls that are so typical of the trails of bubbles in the so-called ‘magnetic field’ of the apparatus, as can be seen in the viewing panes and photographs produced at CERN, for example $^{[102]}$. All those curvilinear tracks, ascribed to the influence of magnets or whatever, are typical of trajectories in an angular momentum system of one kind or another, as opposed to rectilinear ‘ballistic’ trajectories in the Euclidean ‘inertial space’ envisaged by Newton.

    In short, like all other physical phenomena without exception, these phenomena of the tracks in the hydrogen tanks at places like CERN, the vapour trails of cosmic-ray particles in cloud-chambers and comet plumes all reduce, ultimately, without exception, to cinematic quantum events. That is to say, in the final analysis, whichever way we choose to think of them, whether as particle interactions, wave interferences, field-influences or whatever, and regardless of how unfamiliar it may be to think in any other way, these physical phenomena are all, in the end, no more nor less than observational projections out of cinematic sequences of what we have called quantum pixels. In the same way, then, that there are no parameters to describe the motions of ‘electrons’ between energy-levels in the atom, there are no parameters to describe the motions of these ions completely in vacuo between one distant assignation and another even in the very long vacuum-tubes of the ion-colliders in places like CERN and Fermilab. As already discussed, it is only in their actual manifestations, in interactions with one another, as in the detection-systems at the ends of the tubes, that these various ‘particles’ become real. It needs to be stressed, then, that in the seven or so miles in between the source and the detector their existence is completely indeterminate, other than as functions of pure probabilities.

    But, again, why is all this so mysterious? We claim that it is because, as stated in Chapter 9, section 9.3., standard physics constrains us, confusingly, to use the same language to describe things and situations which are completely different. For instance, in the one language category, that of ordinary physical phenomena, it is perfectly sensible to talk about distance and speed, whereas in the other, the category of cinematic quantum sequentiality, there is no such thing as either distance or speed in the ordinary sense of those words. To maintain a clear-cut distinction between these various language categories, therefore, is to realise what traditional physics fails to realise, that certain physical phenomena which are inexplicable on the ordinary physical level may be perfectly explained in terms of events at the quantum level, whereas mixing these concept-categories together, as in extant physics, creates nothing but confusion (illicit conflation). Maintaining an awareness of this category distinction removes much of the mystification that is endemic in our current physical explanations of ‘strange phenomena’ by keeping the languages strictly confined to their own respective language-categories, each to its own distinct realm of signification. As already mentioned, these are the ‘language-games’ that Wittgenstein described, that is, those uses of language which are appropriate to the different categories. In short, it cannot be over-stressed that the language we use to describe physical phenomena and the language we use to describe their quantum-cinematic underpinnings, while they may be complementary are nevertheless distinct and cannot sensibly be conflated in the manner of our traditional Physics.

    10.5. Is light bent by ‘gravity’?

    We now turn to the question as to whether light is bent by ‘gravity’ as in claimed by General Relativity. In answering this question, we must remember that in Normal Realism, light does not exist in vacuo, so how can it be ‘bent’? Recall, also, that all physical phenomena such as polarisation, reflection, diffraction, the dispersion and refraction of light in some medium or other are, ultimately, optical projections from directly perceived cinematic sequences of informational patterns of quantum pixels (photo). This is where the paradigm switch takes place between what are virtually two different and incommensurable language-structures, or ‘language-games’. In the one language-game, we traditionally talk about light-pixels as caused by particles of light (‘photons’) emitted from somewhere out in space and travelling freely through the void until they happen to strike our eyes or some other sort of sensor. In the other language-game we talk, instead, about the light-pixels as primary (a priori) and light-geodesics as secondary (a posteriori) backwards-in-time projections from directly perceived informational patterns and sequences of these directly perceived light-pixels. This is again reminiscent of what was said in Chapter 3, section 3.1, about how the ancient Greek scientists originally thought of light as some kind of illumination projected by the eye itself, which turns the history of light-theories full circle.

    How, then, in that ‘flipside’ language-paradigm of Normal Realism should we explain the phenomenon of what, in the traditional language of physics, are called the ‘effects of gravitational fields on light-beams’? Well, firstly we have to remind ourselves that in Normal Realism there are no unconsummated interactions between atoms, which makes the ‘interaction’ between two atoms an event occurring to both of those atoms at once – as a quantum pixel, that is, which is the energy-differential manifest in the reciprocal quantum jump between the two cinematic still-frames in both atoms. In that case, there is nothing between those interacting atoms that can be bent, twisted or otherwise acted upon in any other way between a light-source and the observer. Secondly we recall that in Normal Realism, the World of physical phenomena is an objective communal projection, as described in Chapter 7, section 7.1. In that objective manifold of interlinked polar dimensions, all spaces are defined in the Machian way, as observed distances between macroscopic bodies. In the Neo-Machian paradigm of Normal Realism the phenomenon of space as we know it objectively – that is, in polar dimensions (see Chapter 7, section 7.1) – is defined in terms of angular momentum as, simplistically, the distance between force-free orbiting bodies. In Normal Realism, this angular momentum metric, in all its complexity is simply the World as we know it, the World of non-linear and ultimately quantised angular momentum.

    Plainly, then, this Real World cannot be of the traditional Euclidean kind. This four-dimensional metric is what might best be thought of as a distorted matrix of dimensions, somewhat like the ‘warped’ space-time of General Relativity. The situation thus described might well be regarded as a merging of the space-time of Normal Realism with that of General Relativity. Also, as we have seen, it is a merging of General Relativity and Quantum Theory. This, of course, is because in contrast to the determinate ‘space-time continuum’ of General Relativity, the space-time of Normal Realism is an indeterminate dis-continuum, or quantum discretum, in which all atomic interactions are proper time-instantaneous, as befits the description of the quantum pixel.

    10.5.1. The non-rectilinear space-time discontinuum

    Clearly, in this objective phenomenal scenario, since any idea of a bent or twisted vacuum, as such, makes no sense, there can be no bending or twisting of ‘space-time itself’, as in General Relativity. In the Neo-Machian thesis of Normal Realism, therefore, the only such bendings or twistings that can be sensibly contemplated have to be those of the actual orbital trajectories of the bodies of which that space-time consists. The geometry of the space-time angular momentum matrix thus defined is certainly not Euclidean. In the presence of large bodies, such as the Sun, say, or some other massive body, these geodesics determined by angular momentum, extrapolated in a manner similar to that of General Relativity, are distorted like the grain around the knots in a plank of wood; the larger the knots, the greater the distortion.

    However, as for the question of whether light is ‘bent by gravity’ in this way, that question is a non-starter. For instance, in Normal Realism there is no way whatsoever of observing or detecting what light is or does in vacuo between its source and sink. Moreover, the notion of ‘light-bending’ by gravitational effects is based on a rather obvious fallacy. This is the assumption that as a quantum of energy, that energy divided by $c^2$ is a mass – i.e., a ‘photon’ – which is customarily said to be ‘attracted by gravity’ like any other mass. However as already discussed in Chapter 3, section 3.2., if a light quantum, assumed stationary – which, of course, it never is – possessed even the tiniest amount of mass, then at its alleged ‘speed $c$’ that mass would be infinite, which makes complete nonsense of any notion of a light-ray as a stream of photons being ‘bent by gravity’. Plainly, then, a light-quantum cannot have any mass, which means that it cannot be correctly described as a particle. In that case, having no mass or materiality, it is not the sort of thing that can be acted upon by ‘gravity’ or anything else between its source and sink. Nor can it be thought of as something travelling in any sort of trajectory. In short, the alleged light-particle called the ‘photon’ cannot possibly exist as such (see Chapter 6, section 6.1).

    As for the purported ‘proof’ of the bending of light around the Sun, which was ostensibly revealed by the photographs taken by Eddington during the solar eclipses of 1919 and 1922, it is now by no means clear as to whether or not these photographs do, in fact, support Einstein’s prediction of light-bending in the way that was so enthusiastically heralded at the time. In any case, the notion of starlight passing at a ‘grazing angle’ to the Sun’s surface seems strange, considering the depth and density of the Sun’s atmosphere. The Sun has nothing like the well-defined surface of, say a billiard ball or ball-bearing. Its atmosphere extends far out into space, beyond the outermost planets up to what is called the heliopause. This means that the photographs were taken through the Sun’s atmosphere and hence are, at best, evidence of the ordinary phenomenon of the refraction of light in a medium. Indeed, all things considered, it would be truly strange if the starlight were not ‘bent’ (refracted) in that way. In fact, calculations carried out in POAMS show that due to refraction through the medium surrounding a star, a ‘light ray’ (meaning, in Normal Realist terms, a sequence of projected quantum illuminations) will be deflected through an angle numerically equivalent to that predicted by General Relativity$^{[103]}$.

    In any case, there have been many challenges made to the experimental evidence for the ‘bending of light’ over the years since 1922 on empirical grounds. On purely logical grounds, however, we can positively predict that no true evidence of this supposed phenomenon of ‘gravitational light-bending’ will ever be found.

    This prediction may be added to another of our firm convictions that any claims to have created the Higgs boson (the so-named ‘God particle’) with the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) at CERN must be erroneous. Other positive predictions of our Normal Realist thesis are that there will never be any real evidence of ‘gravity waves’ or ‘gravitons’, of ‘WIMPS’ (Weakly Interacting Mass Particles), ‘dark matter’ or ‘dark energy’$^{[104]}$ or ‘dark’ anything. These predictions are offered as a test of our thesis according to Popper’s criterion of falsifiability for a truly scientific theory. Hence, we believe that all the expensive attempts to prove the existence of these chimeras must fail – as, indeed, they all have, so far, over about the last sixty or so years up to this present time. It needs to be emphasised, however, that to prove that light is not ‘bent by gravity’ is by no means logically the same as proving Relativity wrong, especially since, as we have seen on the practical level, the theory is better served without that ‘light-travelling’ assumption.

    10.6. ‘Atom smashing’

    The historical prevalence of the Democritan ‘solid-throughout’ conception of the atom has led, in conventional particle physics, to the facile notion of ‘atom smashing’ – yet another example of category confusion. In the atomic research laboratories attempts have continued, by dint of producing higher and higher ‘collision energies’, to ‘shatter’ these atoms into more and more ‘fundamental fragments’ by ‘crashing’ atomic particles into one another at great speed. In the Large Hadron Collider at CERN, these particles are aimed head-on into one another with combined speeds far greater than could ever be achieved with the earlier accelerators. (Relativity forbids, however, that these speeds, whether on their own or in combination, can ever reach, far less exceed, the asymptotic ‘speed-limit’ c.)

    It is well-known that every attempt so far, to ‘shatter’ these particles has succeeded only in creating highly energetic new ones with more and more exotic classifications, so that it has become far beyond the capacity of any normal use of ordinary language to cope with them. (A colleague of ours, the late Alan Smart, once joked that the size of the particles discovered in these laboratories seems to be inversely related to the size of their electricity bill.) Examples of these exotic creations are ‘positrons’ and ‘quarks’, with defining properties of ‘strangeness’, ‘nakedness’, ‘upness’, ‘downness’, ‘charm’, ‘beauty’, etc. These words signify nothing even remotely resembling what they mean in ordinary everyday parlance. Many commentators, including the former British Prime Minister-cum-Scientist, Margaret Thatcher, have regarded it as a scandal that so much international funding is continually wasted on what seems to be altogether unproductive researches, which have failed to advance our commonsense understanding of nature in the least. The most that has been achieved, if anything, is more and more conceptual confusion, which is why, in the 1980s, Thatcher slashed the UK’s contribution to the funding of CERN because, as a scientist herself, she saw this sort of physics as being, as she said, of ‘no practical use’.

    So far as Normal Realism is concerned, these acclaimed ‘new particles’, customarily regarded as scatter-products of ‘particle collisions’, are not bits that have been locked-up inside atoms like grains inside pebbles. They are no more, in the end, than just harmonics and sub-harmonics struck from basic angular momentum configurations, analogous to the way in which striking a bell or plucking a harp-string produces musical tones and overtones. This takes us back to the 6$^{th}$ century B.C. Pythagorean concept of ‘the music of the spheres’, according to which atomic research is more akin to bell-ringing and harp-playing than ‘atom-cracking’.

    From the standpoint of POAMS, then, the simplistic idea of cracking open atoms to reveal their insides is no more than a conceptual throwback to the Democritan conception of atoms as ultimately solid and indestructible bits of matter. In our alternative to that traditional paradigm, what really happens in these ‘atom-smashing’ laboratories is nothing like what that use – or, rather, misuse – of language suggests. Whatever natural subdivisions of the overall angular momentum nexus there might be are there for the taking, not as material fragments but as resonances, harmonics or overtones which experimentation may well uncover, like the sounds which are created when, typically, a wine-glass is tapped with a spoon or stroked around the rim with a finger. In this way, all sorts of so-called ‘subatomic particles’ (harmonics of angular momentum) are created. Some physicists, of Rationalist persuasion, believe that a complete list of these ‘particles’ (resonances) will eventually be revealed, not by applying destructive force but by a study of pure mathematics, in the manner projected by the Pythagoreans [105]. Others, of a more Empiricist mentality, prefer to rely on the heavily funded method employed by the high energy atomic research laboratories – sometimes called the ‘poke-and hope’, or ‘smash-and-crash’ approach to atomic physics.

    Whilst disdaining neither of these methods, per se, the Normal Realist approach is very different. For instance, as already discussed, whereas both of these approaches are reductionist, Normal Realism is essentially holistic [106]. That is to say, we may recall that while the reductionist, ‘bottom-up’ approach is to start with pure precepts about ‘atomic particles’ (or else, pure numbers) and then build up theoretically from there towards an explanation of the physical world, the opposite, ‘top-down’, holistic, approach of Normal Realism is to start from the physical world – that is, with what there is on the ordinary phenomenal or sensory-instrumental level – and ‘drill down’ from there towards the fundamental limits of reduction that logic tells us there must be in the nature of things if anything like an Eleatic stasis is to be avoided [107].

    10.7. The overall integrity of angular momentum systems

    The fact that all parts of a complex angular momentum system are integrated is illustrated by the executive toy shown in Plate 4. All parts of the device are designed to move separately and independently.
