Indeed, it might well be the reason why there seems to be something wrong with the dynamics applied by NASA to their spinning space-probes: that their calculated ‘gravitational’ trajectories essentially take no account of spin. If this is not the reason for the anomaly, then it seems plain that there is no other natural reason known to contemporary physics for these deviant trajectories, since it is an all-attested fact that not only does Newtonian theory fail to solve the Pioneer problem but so also does every other known theory.

    Why, then, despite the most strenuous and imaginative efforts of theoreticians to explain the anomaly in these standard-physics terms, has no simple logical solution to that orbital ‘anomaly’ been found? Could it be because the solution is so simple as to be overlooked, or might it be that Newton’s ‘gravitational’ theory has become so traditionally ingrained and sanctified that no further radical change in it can be contemplated? As history attests, it would not be the first time that scientific progress was inhibited by an over-attachment to old and redundant precepts.

    In contrast to all this, in POAMS the solution to the Pioneer anomaly seems perfectly plain and straightforward. It is simply that the ‘anomaly’ inheres nowhere but in the Newtonian theory, which essentially takes no account of any spin angular momentum that an orbiting body might possess. In the present highly charged atmosphere of theoretical extremism, a simple logical solution of this sort might well be overlooked, being perhaps too ‘simplistic’ to warrant serious consideration. On the other hand, like so many such simple solutions in the past, it might be regarded as a ‘heresy’. Why a ‘heresy’? Because including the angular momentum of spin in the total angular momentum of an orbiting body would commit the sacrilege of making the so-called ‘gravitational constant’ $G$ a variable, which suggestion is, in itself, as heretical as it gets. Worse still for POAMS is that this idea of ‘varying $G$’ is an integral part of a whole paradigm departure from standard orthodox physics – a whole ‘new physics’ in effect, as any diligent reader of this book must surely realise.

    As it transpires, however, POAMS is not the only party to this heresy of suggesting a variable $G$. Prof. M. Milgrom of the Weizmann Institute of Science in Israel has become noted for his proposal of Modified Newtonian Dynamics (MOND) as an alternative to ‘dark matter’. In 1981, he suggested that Newton’s Second Law needs to be modified for very small accelerations. Milgrom’s MOND proposal of varying $G$, however, much as it is similar to POAMS, is not based on the missing spins of the Pioneer probes or of the hosts of astronomical objects, as in POAMS. His variations in $G$ are assumed, ad hoc, to be for different parts of the universe – for which no cogent explanation is given, far less from any identifiable philosophical paradigm. In POAMS, by contrast, the variations in $G$ are universally applied to spinning bodies in general as an explanation of both the ‘missing mass’ anomaly and the ‘Pioneer anomaly’, the only modification needed for Newton’s formalism being for it to include the angular momentum of spin in the total angular momentum of orbiting bodies.

    In POAMS, then, regardless of the shape of a NASA space-probe, or in whatever system of reference it is observed, barring any outside interference en route, the spin angular momentum imparted to it at launch must remain the same throughout the rest of its journey. From this it follows that whatever the amount of spin angular momentum the probe is given, this must affect the orbital angular momentum. And since the magnitude of that orbital angular momentum determines the body’s distance from the centre of mass (barycentre), any spin angular momentum the space-probe may have, regardless of its directional orientation, changes its trajectory relative to the centre of orbit, be this the Earth, the Sun, Jupiter, the centre of a spiral galaxy or whatever.

    In summary, then, with our non-Newtonian inclusion of spin angular momentum in the total angular momentum of a solar orbiting space-probe, the greater the amount of spin, the greater has to be the deviation of its trajectory towards the Sun. This same tendency of spinning bodies to orbit closer to their orbital barycentres explains the ‘anomalous’ compaction of mass towards the centres of spiral galaxies in the way that has already been discussed. This is precisely what has been observed in astronomy and in trajectories of space probes, leading to what NASA’s spokesman, John Anderson, suggested as the possible need for ‘new physics’.

    In Normal Realism, not only do we find a natural logical solution to these recalcitrant so-called ‘anomalies’, but we also find a solution to the seemingly endless search for that theoretical ideal, the ‘Holy Grail’ of contemporary physics, which physicists have projected as the ‘Unified Field’. In Normal Realism, this search terminates at a single stroke by simply cutting the ‘Gordian Knot’ of all classically conceived ‘field-force’ conceptions. This, as we have seen, is by dispensing uniformly with all these mysterious in vacuo processes such as the imagined conduction of light, ‘gravitation’ and other similarly conceived ‘forces’, together with their associated ‘fields’, ‘æthers’ and so on. Is this, perhaps, the ‘new physics’ we are looking for?

    Nor is this the only expression of that felt need for a whole ‘new physics’. In a BBC ‘In Our Time’ programme hosting a number of science authorities [142], Melvyn Bragg, posed the question:

    ‘Newton, for centuries, dominated and directed thought in physics. Then, along came Einstein. He didn’t displace Newton. Do you think it possible that there will be as great a shift by another group of people or movement in physics?’

    The response to this suggestion was:

    ‘Not only is it possible, but it is absolutely necessary. Maybe a new Einstein or a new Newton is going to come along and then we’ll realise how silly we’ve all been.’

    Then, in the Daily Mail, in a Science Blog wryly entitled ‘When Will We Know the Mind of God?’ Michael Hanlon stated:

    ‘The mega machines (such as the lauded Large Hadron Collider at CERN) may yet be humbled by some genius working with a pencil and sheet of paper…We need more of this thinking.’

    Now it may, of course, be no more than sheer coincidence, but this is precisely how Normal Realism and its physics offshoot POAMS started with a young non-academic telephone lineman’s pencilled diagrams following his short private correspondence with Einstein in 1954 [79].

    APPENDIX 7

    Linguistic Analysis

    The Linguistic Movement, so called, stems from Ernst Mach’s philosophical teaching that all experience, in science as in commonsense, is based on what he called ‘sense-data’. This includes the data supplied by instruments of one kind or another, both ordinary and scientific, such as spectacles, mirrors, galvanometers, Geiger counters, seismographs and so on. Indeed, every object is an instrument in his sense. This, of course, was the root of Einstein’s Theory of Relativity, which makes the very existence of physical objects, their dimensions of length, duration and even their mass – as well as other defining properties and qualities, such as density, solidity, etc., relative to a specified observer.

    In the same way that Mach was the forerunner of Einstein in the matter of relativity, Mach’s forerunner in this same line of thinking was Bishop George Berkeley (1685-1753) who was the first to stress this dependence of physical existence on the ‘observer’. Berkeley’s view, however, ran into trouble on account of his notorious and ill-considered dictum esse is percipi (to be is to be perceived), which seemed, unfortunately, to imply that the material world is merely a figment of one’s imagination. This is known as solipsism, the notion that nothing is physically real, that it is all some kind of illusion, or dream. (Indeed, some modern critics of Einstein and Relativity, despite all the many proofs of that theory’s validity, reject it out of hand simply for fear of that imaginary consequence.)

    But, of course, there is no question of Relativity implying solipsism because there can be no observation which is an entirely private matter. All observation, in all sectors of commonsense and science is an essentially communal affair, involving other observers and their perspectives on things, all communicated in spoken, written, behavioural and instrumental language. This is where the Linguistic dimension comes into play, that is, in the codified classifying, dividing and categorising of sensory and instrumental data or phenomena in common language.

    Some of the earlier thinkers in this phenomenalist tradition initiated by Berkeley and Mach sought to codify scientific language in terms of mathematical logic, on the assumption that there are, in nature, fundamental bits of data which are unquestionably veridical – that is, what they called ‘incorrigible’ in the belief that they are the same in our observations of physical objects as in those objects themselves. All one had to do, therefore, according to this view, was to dump ordinary language as being too ‘fluffy’ and imprecise for science, and to build-up a ‘meta-language’ of exact, uninterpreted non-theoretical descriptions of the most elemental bits of observational data and then reconstruct scientific language purely along lines of formal logic and mathematics. In this way, it was supposed, an exact, absolutely objective scientific language, purged of all illusion and subjectivity, and therefore perfectly representative of nature, could be obtained.

    The precursor of this approach was Ludwig Wittgenstein, in his earlier ‘Logical Positivism’ phase, which gained quite a large following among philosophy students of that time. Unfortunately for that movement, it was soon found that as an approach to science it was completely unworkable and had to be abandoned. This was realised eventually by Wittgenstein himself who, after a sojourn alone in the Black Forest, on return to academia and the following he had collected, declared himself, with engaging honesty, to have become ‘the first Non-Wittgensteinian’. As a result, the next phase in Linguistic Philosophy was to recognise that all classifications and divisions of things in language, even scientific language, are essentially and unavoidably contextual. Perception theorists know that even the most elemental stimuli are responded to as integral parts of perceptual wholes, called Gestalten. The same applies to sense-data and/or instrument data in the context of scientific language. That is to say, there can be no language of the sort imagined by the Logical Positivists, which has point-to-point correspondence with any kind of supposed elemental data.

    Realising this contextual nature of meaning in language is what led, by reaction, to the next phase in the Linguistic Movement. This was to regard what its supporters called Ordinary Language as the ultimate arbiter of truth and reality. (This was, by default, the classical ‘King’s English’ of the Oxbridge elite.) In universities where these departments of Philosophy and Science came together, they were ‘strange bedfellows’. The philosophers were anti-science and the scientists were anti-philosophy. The philosophers scorned any kind of technical language as ‘scientism’ and the scientists derided the philosophers as the self-elected ‘Language Police’. The high point of this conflict was exemplified at the University College of Wales, Swansea, where Wittgenstein’s ‘wing-man’ Rush Rhees presided over the Philosophy

    Department. So much at odds were these departments that on one occasion Rhees and a member of the science department actually came to blows over it.

    So far as these philosophers were concerned, this ‘Ordinary Language’ was not to be sullied by ‘jargon’ of any kind – by physics ‘jargon’ in particular. At Swansea, for instance, any talk among students about such things as, say, ‘atoms’ ‘electrons’ and the like – indeed, just about everything with which the Physics Department was concerned – was forbidden by their Philosophy mentor, Rush Rhees. In other universities, during the 1960s, this same Linguistic Movement gained momentum. At Oxford, for example, it was fronted by the likes of John L. Austin and Gilbert Ryle, also, in the U.S.A., by Norman Malcolm, of Cornell University.

    However, this Ordinary Language proved to be every bit as unworkable as its predecessor, Logical Positivism, and in due time it died a natural death. After its heyday in Swansea, during the 1960s, this Linguistic influence led to the development of Normal Realism by the present author, Pope. As an erstwhile telephone engineer prior to university, Pope was out of sympathy with this scholastic anti-science movement. Finally, he signified this, by walking out, conspicuously, in the middle of one of Rush Rhees’ most vigorous diatribes against what he called ‘scientism’.

    For Pope, this Linguistic Movement was basically sound, but after graduating as a mature-age student in Philosophy of Science (at the University College of North Wales, Bangor) he sought to merge the interests of progressive science and commonsense. This cross-disciplinary recombination of academically divided Arts and Science was neither supported nor encouraged by the University. Pope’s version of linguistic philosophy was academically unclassifiable – indeed, as it transpired, it was distinctly ‘heretical’. This was not least insofar as it militated against much of the standardised high-flown kind of pure jargon enjoyed by scientists that Pope regarded as both unnecessary and detrimental to commonsense understanding. His primary aim was to seek out and remove these jargonised over-interpretations of observational and experimental data wherever possible in favour of more logically streamlined and conceptually economical uses of ordinary commonsense language.

    In 1982, this merger of Science and Philosophy was continued by Pope in collaboration with Dr. Anthony Osborne, at Keele University, Staffordshire, UK. In his doctoral thesis, Osborne had already considered, from a mathematical standpoint, the possibility of changes in the ‘gravitational constant’, $G$, and together, he and Pope discovered that an angular momentum formulation alone can explain the orbital motions of bodies from atoms to galaxies without reference to any of the traditionally conceived in vacuo ‘field-forces’ of ‘gravitation’, ‘electrostatics’, ‘magnetostatics’ or whatever. This academically unclassifiable, cross-disciplinary collaboration between exponents of Arts and Science respectively, became known as the Pope-Osborne Angular Momentum Synthesis (POAMS) $^{[6]}$.

    APPENDIX 8

    Concepts for Assimilation

    In summary, the concepts we have presented for consideration in this review are as follows:

    1. In this Neo-Machian prescription for an alternative new physics, called Normal Realism, the emphasis falls on just plain observation, as a foil against what more and more people currently see as the growth of theoretical extremism in modern physics and cosmology. For the natural truth-chaser this means seeking out the very best, most logically consistent interpretations of natural phenomena and encapsulating these findings in the most efficient, most overall-understood, most proper uses of common language, keeping theoretical guesswork and its associated jargon to the barest minimum. So far as Normal Realism is concerned, these phenomena are the ultimate starting-point of any scientific analysis. The radical departure is that in this new approach to physics these fundamental physical phenomena are not mediated by light, as in extant physics, they are light, in informational patterns and sequences of directly observed quantum pixels. In Normal Realism these packages of structured energy, or information, are the first call in our scientific understanding of the physical world, all else being secondary to the presentation and interpretation of this basic information.
    2. As befits this radical, or revolutionary approach to physics, in Normal Realism the constant $c$, commonly called 'the speed of light.' is not the speed of anything but is simply a constant relating conventional units of metres and seconds in the optical projection of distance (186,000 miles to the second in Imperial units, and 300,000 kilometres to the second in Metric). Even the distance-time $s / c$ discovered by Römer is projected (retro-projected) in this way.
    3. Not only does this dispensing with the 'velocity' interpretation of $c$ provide a firm philosophical grounding for physics in an identifiable philosophy (the observational relativism, or phenomenalism, of Mach), it also makes geometrical child's-play of an important aspect of Einstein's Special Relativity. This is by deriving Einstein's little-understood time dilation formula, not from traditional physics precepts but by geometro-temporal means and much more directly, from the altogether more commonly known Pythagoras' Theorem, applied to all four (i.e., three of space plus one of time) dimensions of physical phenomena.
    4. This ahistorical, Pythagorean simplification of the time dilation formula reveals that Relativity is automatically quantised at its very root. That is to say, inverting the time dilation formula to express frequency produces, directly, the basic Balmer-Rydberg formula for the frequencies of the light-spectrum with no necessity for involving the traditional theories of 'electrodynamics', etc.

    5. By this same method of simplification and pure syllogistic, expressing the relativistic time formula in terms of $\nu^2$ and substituting this for the $\nu^2$ in the standard formula for orbital angular momentum turns that time formula into a formula for orbital time dilation as observed in the Pound-Rebka and other experiments, as well as in the GPS and GLONASS satellite systems.

    6. In Normal Realism there is no such thing as Newtonian straight-line linear momentum other than as an ideal special case of angular momentum. This enables us to dispense with Newtonian in vacuo 'gravitational force' in favour of an angular momentum formulation as the explanation of free orbital motion.

    7. The basic universal 'substance' of this new physics is shown to be, not matter (mass) but the integrated mass-length-time of angular momentum as manifest, for example, in the phenomenon of the orbital motions of bodies in our solar system.
    8. Angular momentum is an automatically paired and balanced, instantly reciprocal relation between bodies such as the planets and other astronomical objects, as well as between those 'particles' customarily called 'electrons' and 'protons'. In this alternative new physics, therefore, there is no 'gravity', 'electric (Coulomb) force', 'magnetic force' or any other traditionally conceived in vacuo 'forces' or associated 'fields'. It is all inter-connected angular momentum, of one form or another, at various levels - 'wheels within wheels' as it were - of orbital, spin, oscillatory or whatever.
    9. In this way, angular momentum replaces Newtonian 'gravity' as the universal agency of orbital motion. The restraining of free orbital motion, as for instance, in a body prevented by the surface of a planet from orbiting at a radius smaller than that of the planet's surface, is manifest as a real measurable force, as in what is familiarly known as weight.
    10. Angular momentum is a conserved quantity including orbital, spin, swing (as of a pendulum), vibration and any other form of angular momentum. Conservation therefore demands that for the total amount of angular momentum of an orbiting body, the more there is of spin, or any of those other forms of angular momentum, the less there has to be of orbital angular momentum and vice versa. This provides a natural commonsense explanation of both NASA's Pioneer anomaly and the anomaly of the so-called 'missing mass of the universe', both of which 'anomalies', in Normal Realism, are due to the omission of spin in the 'gravitational' orbital equations applied to those phenomena.
    11. In free orbital motion the direction of the spin is also, gyroscopically, conserved. Superimposed on the magnitude effects of spin on the orbits of bodies are those due to angular (vector) orientation. For the orbits of bodies such as planets and satellites, including NASA's space-probes, these vectorial effects are miniscule in comparison with the magnitude effects overall, though measurable in principle.

    12. The limit of reduction of angular momentum is the irreducible unit, or atom of angular momentum, $h$ (as opposed to $h$ which signifies a unit of energy times time or action). It consists, basically, of two elementary masses orbiting a common barycentre. These ultimate, mutually balanced mass-components of the angular momentum on that fundamental level of analysis are not real solid objects. They are purely formal, with mass-values equivalent to those of the conventional 'electron' and 'proton'. Their commonly conceived 'charges', in coulombs, are cashed-out instead as the kinetic energy of spin in mechanical units of joules (pace Uhlenbeck and Goudsmit).

    13. The common angular momentum barycentres, on all levels, from atoms to galaxies, have, in themselves, no mass nor any other physical properties or qualities, real or imaginary. On the macro-scale, this has led to the fanciful description of these barycentres as 'Black Holes'. In Normal Realism, however, these barycentric 'holes' are not mysterious self-sufficient 'singularities'. They are no more than common characteristics of all angular momentum systems, such as atoms, whirlpools, cyclones and spiral galaxies. Calling them 'Black Holes' is picturesque but invites metaphysical over-elaboration and confusion.

    14. Atoms, in Normal Realism, are not self-sufficient freely moving things-in-themselves. They exist only as analytic products of their environment. They do not exist in themselves, any more than bubbles in a beer glass exist without the beer.

    15. No 'fundamental particles' have the capacity to act or manifest themselves – to exist, even – apart from the environment.

    16. It is thus always within the environment that physical causality resides, not in any sorts of autonomous 'fundamental particles'. In classical physics, all causality is conceived as acting from earlier to later. In Normal Realism, however, as an integral part of physics there are also causes of events that act in reverse. That is, they are naturally ascribed, in probabilistic terms; to the future as in the goal-directed biological functions and behaviours of organisms (Aristotle called these future end-states entelechies).

    17. In the Normal Realist atom ('quantum accumulator') the component masses are not conceived as rotating smoothly around each other; that is, continually like the Moon around the Earth and the Earth around the Sun. On that ultimate micro-physical level of analysis the 'motion', if such it may be called, consists of single, complete orbital cycles which change only from one orbit level to another in discrete quantum jumps or jerks, like those sequences of what are called 'stills' in cinematography.

    18. There are no parameters to describe the transition between these atomic levels in the atom. Each energy-state is discrete, complete and consummate – as we say, like the jump between one cinematographic 'still' and another. In Normal Realism, therefore, all physical action consists of the sequencing of these 'quantum stills'. That is to say, the action is always between those stills, not within those 'stills' as such.

    19. The quantum jumps between angular momentum energy-levels within the atom (quantum accumulator) are measured in integer units of $h$ in the jumps between the quantum stills. The energy-difference between levels is released or absorbed in disembodied action units $h$, which are the quantum 'pixels' that are manifest in the colours of the spectrograph (see Appendices 2 and 3). In more complex patterns and sequences of the same, they are the optical information out of which whole physical scenarios are projected by the observer. All else, right down to atoms and their formal constituents, is logically extrapolated from that light-information on the macro-level of physical phenomena. We repeat that other than in the contexts of these macro-physical phenomena, those micro-physical entities we call 'atoms', 'electrons' and so on do not exist.

    20. In Normal Realism, existence is not the metaphysical 'something-I-know-not-what', which it is notoriously impossible to define. It is simply an integral part of the function of language. 'Existence' is bestowed on the subject of a sentence by use of some variant or other of the verb 'to be' (e.g., 'is', 'was', 'were', 'will be'... etc.). For instance, to say 'electrons exist' is no more than to say that in some theoretical system which Wittgenstein calls a particular 'language-game' there are (putatively), things which are commonly known as 'electrons'. However, things which exist in one 'language-game' may not exist in another. The 'æther' for instance, which existed in classical physics, does not exist in modern relativistic language. Nor does 'gravity', which exists in Newtonian and relativistic language, exist in the language of Normal Realism. There is no sensible idea of an 'existence' which is absolute – that is, common to all possible language systems in the same way, independently of language.

    21. All atomic energy-transactions on the quantum level are proper-time-instantaneous resonances which take place in accordance with Newton's law of equal and opposite action/reaction. There can be no such thing as a one-ended or time delayed quantum jump any more than there can be a one-ended coil of wire or ball of string. All atomic interactions are complete, immediate and consummate regardless of how distance appears to us on the macro-phenomenal level. Nor is there any causality at the quantum level. All causality belongs on the macro-phenomenal level and takes place only in statistical numbers of atomic events in accordance with the statistical Second Law of Thermodynamics (Entropy).

    22. 'Atom smashing' traditionally so called, is a misnomer. There are no ultimate, solid-all-through material particles of the sort that Democritus imagined, to be 'smashed' apart. The 'insides' of these entities are emptinesses rather than substances. This presents a theoretical inversion, or 'photonegative', as it were, of Democritan atomism insofar as it puts material substance on the outsides of atoms and the void on their insides. In Normal Realism, the 'subatomic particles' claimed to be the 'scatter-products' of particle collisions are not the inner constituents of atoms, ready to be revealed, like the kernels of nuts. These 'components' of atoms are no more than harmonics and sub-harmonics of the irreducible unit of angular momentum, $\hbar$, created in these experiments in the way that tones, overtones and so on, are created by, say, tapping a wineglass or striking a bell.

    From the Normal Realist point of view, then, as explained in this thesis, Democritan materialism is like a key that Science has used upside-down for millennia in trying, unsuccessfully, to unlock the secrets of nature. Our philosophical inversion of that materialistic physics and ethics proposes that we turn that upside-down key the right way up, so as to use it in the proper way to open the door to a commonsense understanding of all natural phenomena, including life and humanity, all in a seamless scientific and philosophical continuum which knows no academic apartheid between Science and Arts, hence no voodoo chalk line to be observed between the studies of Physics and Philosophy.

Experimental evidence of the existence of these theoretical entities called 'atoms' was not advanced until the beginning of the 20th century, when it became the dominant theory of subsequent physics.

Our senses, he argued, are known to fail us, and since they do so on some occasions they may do so on all occasions, hence cannot, in themselves be trusted. But what physical reality is, in itself, behind our sense-perceptions, he decided, is certainly known to God, and since it is God who designed and made our senses, then since God is 'no deceiver', what we perceive in our clearest moments must be 'what God perceives'. So, he concluded, what we perceive of this God's-eye-view reality in our most clear-eyed and clear-minded moments of perception must be a perfect mental facsimile of that true, albeit remote, physical reality. In this way, Descartes gives us two realities for the price of one – the one which God perceives and the other which we perceive, and which by the power of true reason are made not two things but one.

Some time later, Einstein abandoned the concept of the ‘photon’, which has since survived only vestigially in the minds of some of his followers and the popular media.

One commentator likens the concept of the photon in flight to that of ‘one hand clapping’, and the 17$^{th}$ century empiricist philosopher John Locke compared the ballistic concept of light (as it then was) as akin to that of ‘fairies with racquets knocking tiny tennis balls about, some of which incidentally strike men’s foreheads (and eyes) while most of them pass by’.

The refraction of the liquid increased with the speed of the liquid flow, showing that relative to the liquid, the ‘slowing’ of the light increases with the kinetic energy of the molecules of the liquid. It was found, by this means, that the speed of the molecules and the resulting refraction exactly fitted the relativistic time dilation formula.

    80 In a letter to Pope, in 1954, this was confirmed by Albert Einstein in answer to this very question. Einstein showed, in effect, that when two observers, $A$ and $B$, move apart, each one’s time is equally time-dilated relatively to the other, but only up to the point where one or the other of them, say $A$, reverses his motion to bring them back together. In undertaking that reversing action it is not sufficient for $A$ simply to put his engine into reverse, which would serve only to cancel his outward momentum and bring him to rest in $B$’s observer-frame. To complete the reversal he would have to abandon that state of rest by turning his ship around and blasting off towards $B$ with the same speed as that of his outward motion but in the opposite direction. The effect of this two-stage operation, therefore, would not be simply to cancel his outward momentum in $B$’s observer-frame but to double it, which makes his inward motion in that frame in no way symmetrical with that of his outward motion. Eventually, thinking it over, Pope became convinced of this rational explanation.

    86 In quantum mechanics, angular momentum is quantised – that is, it cannot vary continuously, but only in ‘quantum leaps’ between certain allowed values. The orbital angular momentum of a subatomic particle is always a whole-number multiple of $h$, known as the reduced Planck’s constant, defined as that constant divided by $2\pi$.

    87 In 1960, Robert Pound and Glen Rebka of Harvard University succeeded in testing a crucial prediction of General Relativity, that the frequency of light is affected by gravity. They reported that Pound, at the top of a tower in a Harvard physics building, in phone contact with Rebka in the basement, actually measured this very tiny predicted change in what was curiously called ‘the weight of light’. For this they had to find a source of light radiation whose frequency was known with enormous precision, making it possible to measure the slightest phase-difference between the two sources. For this they employed the Mössbauer Effect, which is the measurable phase-difference in sources of gamma radiation. The same prediction is made in POAMS as in General Relativity but, of course, without any reference to ‘gravitation’ or the ‘weight of light’.

    $^{91}$ Recall that in Normal Realism there is no such thing as Newton’s in vacuo ‘gravitational force’ responsible for orbital motion.     $^{92}$ As measures of energy, static measures in coulombs and kinetic measures in joules are inter-transformable. In this way, the postulated static ‘charge’ on an ‘electron’ or a ‘proton’ can be cashed out kinetically as a spin angular momentum of $\frac{1}{2} h$. (Osborne, A. D. and Pope, N. V., ‘An Angular Momentum Synthesis of “Gravitational” and “Electrostatic” Forces,’ Galilean Electrodynamics 14, Special Issue 1, Spring 2003, pp 9-19.)

    $^{93}$ The hypothesis that the electron considered as an elementary particle, has an intrinsic angular momentum, as though it were spinning, was first introduced by Uhlenbeck and Goudsmit in 1926. This was not known to Bohr when he proposed his model for the atom in 1913. According to our approach, it is this spin angular momentum that provides the correct parameters for the hydrogen atom without assuming the existence of an electrostatic ‘force’.

    $^{94}$ Whereas the ‘gravitational’ constant is in the order of $10^{-11}$, the ‘electronic’ constant, in those same dimensions, has the prodigiously larger order of $10^{29}$. This is due to the spin angular momentum of these micro-physical objects being so large in comparison with their orbital angular momentum. Macroscopic objects with that proportion of spin to orbit would disintegrate long before reaching that level. (See reference 64, Chapter 7.)

    $^{96}$ It is well known that in Relativity there are two times by which a speed can be measured. One is the observational distance $s$ in metres, travelled by an object in a time $t$ registered by the body itself as seen by the home observer, called the proper time, and the other is that same distance in metres as registered in the time $t_R$ of the motion as measured by the home observer’s own clocks, called the relative time. A common and most fundamental mistake is to think that in order to measure the proper time of a body, one has somehow to travel, as it were, with the body itself. This is not so. That proper time, as recorded by the body itself, can be read by any observer of the motion anywhere, and is the same for all observers of that motion; that is to say, one does not have to be actually with a clock in order to read the time it records. In other words, in the view of any observer, both times, the proper time and the relative time are part of the same observation. There should be nothing contradictory or intrinsically puzzling about this, as many people seem to imagine. This is why any body has two speeds not one, as is traditionally imagined. The one is the observational distance $s$ travelled by the body in the time $t$ of the body itself, and the other is that same distance $s$ travelled by the body in the relative-time $t_R$ of that same motion. The first of these speeds is unlimited and the second is limited to $c$.

    $^{104}$ From the Linguistic Analysis standpoint, the idea of ‘dark energy’ is pure nonsense. Energy is, by definition a measure of motion, and motion makes no sense other than in relation to a body. The idea of ‘dark energy’ is therefore as nonsensical as talking about ‘dark motion’ or ‘dark velocity’.

    $^{109}$ Something needs to be said here about what is reported by the BBC, on its News Programme, Nov. 8th, 2010, as the successful creation of a miniature Big Bang in the Large Hadron Collider laboratory at CERN. Deeper inspection reveals, however, that this ‘miniature Big Bang’ is not what was actually discovered but, rather, what was expected to be discovered. What has actually been discovered, stripped of all the speculative theoretical trimmings, is simply a very strong interaction-event with a temperature of ‘a million times hotter than the centre of the Sun.’ Accompanied by speculative computer-graphic images of that event it will doubtless be reported to an expectant public, via the media, that the Hadron Collider experiment at CERN was a success, and that the billions of pounds and dollars spent on it by its sponsors was not wasted. In fact, however, the ‘discovery’ is no more significant than to discover that feeding more fuel into a furnace makes it hotter, or that the harder you hit a bell the louder it rings.

    $^{116}$ Astronomers, following Fritz Zwicky in the 1930s, calculated that there has to be about five times more material in the visible universe than can be accounted for by the Newtonian gravitational equations. This invisible – or, rather, speculative – material, Zwicky named ‘dark matter’. A steadfast belief in the existence of this esoteric material has persisted among scientists despite the fact that over more than seven decades since these calculations were made, all attempts to establish the existence of this anomalous, invisible matter have signally failed.

    $^{117}$ This proposal of varying $G$ was advanced in 1983 by Professor Mordecai Milgrom of the Weizmann Institute. It is a modification of the usual Newtonian force law postulated as an alternative to the hypothesis of Dark Matter. This alternative hypothesis is called MOND (Modified Newtonian dynamics). Both MOND and ‘dark matter’ were invented to explain the presence of mass discrepancies in astronomical data.

    $^{119}$ In 1814 Pierre Simon Laplace (1749-1827), a French mathematician and astronomer, created a hypothetical ‘demon’ who, if it knew precisely the position and motion of every piece of matter in the universe at any time, could calculate exactly the whole future of the universe. In other words, Laplace’s assumption was that ‘the past completely determines the future’. This assumption was finally dispatched by the well-known Heisenberg’s Indeterminacy Principle (1927).

    $^{121}$ Werner Heisenberg (1901-1976). In 1927, Heisenberg discovered that when measuring pairs of observables in a single elementary particle, on increasing the accuracy of the measurement of one quantity he succeeded only in increasing the uncertainty of any simultaneous measurement of the other quantity. The most familiar of these pairs of quantities is the position and momentum of the particle.

    $^{122}$ Tom Van Flandern was an American astronomer, specialising in celestial mechanics who was known as an outspoken proponent of unorthodox views on various topics. He used standard experimental techniques to determine the propagation speed of gravity, which yielded a speed far in excess of light-speed. This, he claimed, is because in contrast to light, gravity has no detectable aberration or propagation-delay for its action. He was also a critic of the Big Bang theory of cosmic creation and the theory of dark matter.

    $^{123}$ Jacques Charles (1746-1823) investigated the relationship between the volume of a gas and how it increases with temperature. His law, called Charles’s Law, states that the volume of a given amount of dry ideal gas is directly proportional to the Kelvin Temperature provided that the amount of gas and the pressure remain fixed. The obverse implication from this is that for a given volume of gas at a given constant temperature, the pressure of the gas evenly spread throughout the volume, is constant.

    141 It seems that one of the first suggestions of including spin in the total angular momentum of an orbiting body was in the early eighties, in a research project at Keele University, UK. Many publications of this idea have since appeared in the name of POAMS. So far, the latest publication on this theme is a book written mainly for graduate and post-graduate Mathematics students, entitled, Light-Speed, Gravitation and Quantum Instantaneity by A. D. Osborne and N. V. Pope (2007). In that book it is shown how spin angular momentum combines with orbital angular momentum in increasing or decreasing the orbit radius in a body orbiting according to Newtonian ‘gravitational’ dynamics. This was, arguably, the first theoretical intimation of ‘varying G’ as the result of the inclusion of spin angular momentum in the total angular momentum of an orbiting body.
